L23: 1. Executive Summary
L24: 1.1. Application Introduction
L25: On October 15, 2021, Omeros Corporation (the Applicant) submitted a Biologics License Application (BLA) 761152 for Yartemlea (narsoplimab-wuug), a monoclonal antibody (mAb) that inhibits mannan-binding lectin-associated serine protease 2 (MASP-2), the effector enzyme of the lectin pathway of complement for the proposed indication of the treatment of patients with hematopoietic stem cell transplant-associated thrombotic microangiopathy (TA-TMA). The proposed dosage of narsoplimab is 4 mg/kg administered as an intravenous infusion once a week (b) (4). On October 15, 2021, the Agency issued a Complete Response Letter (CRL). On March 26, 2025, the Applicant submitted a complete response to the CRL, seeking approval for narsoplimab for the treatment of patients with hematopoietic stem cell transplantation thrombotic microangiopathy (TA-TMA). For the purposes of this review, narsoplimab-wuug will be referred to as narsoplimab.
L26: The basis of the CR was that the data submitted as part of the original BLA failed to demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness for narsoplimab for the proposed indication. For details regarding the specific review issues identified during initial review and contributing to the decision to issue a CRL see Section 0. For complete details of the original review see summary review uploaded to DARRTS on October 15, 2021. For a brief summary of the regulatory history see Section 2 of this review.
L27: New information submitted with this resubmission include:
L28: • Response to Deficiencies Cited in the Complete Response Letter, External Control Data, and Summary• Compassionate Use Expanded Access Data which included pediatric patients
L29: On May 20, 2025, and May 27, 2025, the Applicant submitted new data from the external control, which included individual patient data registered on the Kyoto Stem Cell Transplantation Group (KSCTG) registry. This data had not been previously submitted to or reviewed by the Agency, on July 10, 2025; therefore, the Agency notified the Applicant that these submissions constituted a major amendment to the application and that the goal date would be extended by three months to the current PDUFA date, in order to provide time for a full review of the new data.
L30: Based on the data included with this resubmission, the entire multidisciplinary review team recommends approval of narsoplimab for the treatment of adult and pediatric patients two years of age and older with hematopoietic stem cell transplant-associated thrombotic
L31: • Greater than or equal to 50kg: 370mg by intravenous infusion over 30 minutes once weekly. Increase frequency to twice weekly if there is inadequate improvement in TA-TMA signs and symptoms.• Less than 50kg: 4mg/kg by intravenous infusion over 30 minutes once weekly. Increase frequency to twice weekly if there is inadequate improvement in TA-TMA signs and symptoms.
L32: 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE)
L33: Substantial evidence of effectiveness (SEE) of narsoplimab in adults with TA-TMA was established based on one adequate and well controlled clinical investigation with confirmatory evidence. The adequate and well-controlled investigation is Study OMS-721-TMA-001 (Study TMA-001). Confirmatory evidence was provided by efficacy data from patients treated under an expanded access program for whom patient level data was available (EAP Subgroup 1).
L34: Study TMA-001 is a single-arm, multicenter trial of narsoplimab in adult patients with TA-TMA, with a control provided by a historical comparator rate in untreated patients based on the literature.
L35: Study TMA-001 enrolled 28 patients with TA-TMA if they had diagnosis of TA-TMA that persisted at least 2 weeks following modification or discontinuation of treatment with calcineurin inhibitors (CNI) or at least 30 days after transplantation, with platelet count &lt;150,000/\mu\text{L}, evidence of microangiopathic hemolysis (presence of schistocytes, serum LDH &gt; upper limit of normal (ULN), or haptoglobin &lt; lower limit of normal), and renal dysfunction. All patients also met criteria for high-risk TMA, based on the high-risk criteria as defined by recent proposed consensus guidelines (Schoettler et al., 2023). The primary efficacy endpoint was the proportion of TA-TMA patients who responded to narsoplimab treatment. Treatment response was defined as an improvement in TMA- associated laboratory markers (platelet count and LDH) as well as improvement in clinical status (improvement in organ function at any time point post-baseline during the study or freedom of transfusion).
L36: In Study TMA-001, 17 of 28 patients (61%; 95% CI: 40.6, 78.5) achieved a TMA response. This was compared to a historic response rate of 23.3% (95% CI: 15.1, 34.2), which was based on analysis of 149 patients with TA-TMA identified in the literature who had not received narsoplimab or other pharmacologic treatment for TA-TMA and who had a response in one of the laboratory assessments or a clinical response. The review team concluded that calculating a response rate based on all patients from the selected literature who had not received prior pharmacologic treatment, including those from studies with less stringent response criteria than used in Study TMA-001, would likely overestimate an historic response rate, and therefore could be considered as an acceptable benchmark for study TMA-001 response rate to be
L37: measured against. The TMA-001 response rate of 61% is higher than the selected literature based historic control response rate 23.3% (95% CI: 15.1, 34.2) with non-overlapping confidence intervals.
L38: Confirmatory evidence of effectiveness was demonstrated in 19 patients (13 adult, 6 pediatric) who were treated under the EAP program and for whom patient level data was available (EAP Subgroup 1). The TA-TMA response rate for EAP Subgroup 1 overall was 13/19 (68%), consistent with the findings for the primary endpoint of Study TMA-001.
L39: Substantial evidence of effectiveness for narsoplimab in pediatric patients aged 2 years and older with TA-TMA was based on partial extrapolation of clinical benefit demonstrated in adults. This extrapolation is reasonable given the similarity in the pathophysiology, clinical signs and symptoms, and course of TA-TMA in adults and pediatric patients. Partial extrapolation is supported by response to treatment data from 6 patients in the EAP Subgroup 1 who had baseline disease characteristics and TA-TMA response rates consistent with the adult patients.
L40: Benefit-Risk Framework
L41: DimensionEvidence and UncertaintiesConclusions and Reasons




Analysis of Condition


TA-TMA is a challenging complication of HSCT associated with significant morbidity and mortality. The complex pathophysiology and multiple contributing factors converge on a final pathway involving endothelial cell injury and complement activation. TA-TMA is characterized by microangiopathic hemolytic anemia, thrombocytopenia, elevated serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) and presence of schistocytes on peripheral blood smear. The gold standard for diagnosis is based on characteristic histological tissue findings; however, the high risk for procedural complications and bleeding limits the ability to obtain a tissue diagnosis.
The incidence of TA-TMA varies among studies due to different criteria used for diagnosis. There are several risk factors described in the literature for the development of TA-TMA include older age, female sex, advanced primary cancer disease, unrelated donor transplants, high-dose conditioning regimen, human leukocyte antigen (HLA) mismatch, nonmyeloablative transplants, total body irradiation, calcineurin use, acute graft-versus host disease and infection.
The clinical presentation ranges from mild, self-limited disease to more serious disease involving multi-organ involvement (kidneys, gastrointestinal tract, central nervous system, heart, lung, and serosal surfaces), with severe disease leading to multi-organ failure and death.


TA-TMA is a rare, serious and life-threatening condition. It is a major cause of morbidity and mortality post allogeneic HSCT in both adult and pediatric populations.
L42: DimensionEvidence and UncertaintiesConclusions and Reasons





TA-TMA has a poor prognosis with mortality rates described in literature in excess of 50% (Schoettler et al., 2023).




Current Treatment Options


There are no FDA approved therapies for the treatment of TA-TMA. A range of treatments have been used in this condition but none with established efficacy. Therapeutic interventions include removal of the insulting agent, transfusion support, and treating concurrent conditions such as GVHD and infections. While often considered as an initial step in management, modification and/or discontinuation of calcineurin inhibitors and/or sirolimus may not be effective as there is no clear evidence that discontinuation of these agents provides a benefit to patients with TA-TMA. The role of total plasma exchange in TA-TMA remains unclear and is often not beneficial. Off-label therapies such as defibrotide and rituximab have demonstrated variable response rates and have an unclear impact on mortality. Other complement inhibitors such as C5 inhibitors are currently being investigated and frequently used off-label in treatment of TA-TMA.


There are no approved therapies for pediatric or adult patients with TA-TMA. There is an unmet clinical need for an effective approved therapy for patients with TA-TMA.



Benefit


The benefit of narsoplimab was assessed in a single-arm, externally controlled, open-label study (Study TMA-001), which enrolled 28 patients with TA-TMA who received narsoplimab every week for 4 to 8 weeks. The primary endpoint was response rate, which was defined as improvement in two laboratory TMA markers (LDH and platelet counts) and either improvement in organ function or independence from transfusions.
In Study TMA-001, 17 of 28 patients (61%; 95% CI: 40.6, 78.5) achieved a TMA response. This was compared to an historic


The submitted data demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness on TA-TMA response. The magnitude of response rate to treatment, compared to literature based historic control, demonstrates that narsoplimab is active in this disease.
L43: DimensionEvidence and UncertaintiesConclusions and Reasons




response rate of 23.3% (95% CI: 15.1, 34.2), which was based on analysis of 149 patients with TA-TMA identified in the literature who had not received narsoplimab or other pharmacologic treatment for TA-TMA and who had a response in one of the laboratory assessments or a clinical response.

• Confirmatory evidence came from a group of patients treated under an expanded access (EAP). EAP Subgroup 1 comprised 19 patients (13 adults and 6 pediatric) for which there is patient level information, provided. The response definition was consistent with that in Study TMA-001. In total, 13 of 19 patients with TA-TMA (68.4%, 95% CI: 43.4, 87.4) in EAP Subgroup 1 responded to narsoplimab treatment, including 9 of 13 adult patients (69.2%, 95% CI: 38.6, 90.9) and 4 of 6 pediatric patients (66.7%, 95% CI: 22.3, 95.7).
• In the 6 pediatric patients with subject level data available, the baseline disease characteristics and TMA response rates were consistent with what was observed in the adult population.


Partial extrapolation of efficacy data from the adult population is adequate to support the pediatric indication in pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with TA-TMA because of similar disease pathophysiology.



Risk and Risk Management


• Primary safety profile is based on data from the 28 patients treated with narsoplimab on Study TMA-001.

○ Serious adverse reactions occurred in 61% of patients receiving narsoplimab. Serious adverse reactions in &gt;5% of patients who received narsoplimab included, acute kidney injury, confusional state, acute respiratory failure,




The safety database is limited, as would be expected given the size of the affected population, and the lack of concurrent control. All important or frequent adverse events were presumed to be potentially drug-related for the purposes of labeling and risk mitigation, although it is likely that many of the events were caused by the underlying malignancy or chemotherapy.
The identified risks can be adequately addressed in labeling. To minimize risk, labeling should

L46: 1.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L47: TA-TMA is a rare, serious and life-threatening disease associated with a high mortality rate. There are no approved treatments for this condition in adults or pediatric patients. Thus, TA-TMA represents an unmet medical need.
L48: The benefit of narsoplimab was evaluated in a single-arm, externally controlled, open-label trial (Study TMA-001) of 28 patients with high-risk TA-TMA who received narsoplimab for 4 to 8 weeks, supported by detailed patient-level data for 19 high-risk patients treated under the narsoplimab EAP (EAP Subgroup 1). TMA response, defined as improvement in (i) both of two laboratory TMA markers (LDH levels and platelet counts) and (ii) at least one of two clinical findings, specifically improvement in organ function and freedom from transfusion, was achieved in 17/28 (61%) patients in Study TMA-001 and 13/19 (68.4%) of the EAP Subgroup 1 patients. These results were compared to a threshold based on historical literature review.
L49: The study protocol for Study TMA-001 specified study objectives, enrollment criteria, outcome measures and an analysis plan to evaluate outcomes, which helped inform FDA's determination that the characteristics of an adequate and well-controlled study are present in Study TMA-001.
L50: The primary evidence supporting the safety and efficacy assessment in this resubmission derives from Study TMA-001 submitted with the original BLA along with the new information provided by the Applicant with this submission. Study TMA-001 is a single-arm, multicenter trial of narsoplimab in adult patients with TA-TMA. Study TMA-001 enrolled 28 patients with TA-TMA if they had diagnosis of TA-TMA that persisted at least 2 weeks following modification or discontinuation of CNI treatment or at least 30 days after transplantation with platelet count &lt; 150,000/\muL, evidence of microangiopathic hemolysis (presence of schistocytes, serum LDH &gt; upper limit of normal (ULN), or haptoglobin &lt; lower limit of normal) and renal dysfunction.
L51: During this review, after consideration of the additional submitted data for the 5 patients for which the review team disagreed with the Applicant's assessment as responders during the review of the initial cycle, the Agency verified the Applicant's primary outcome measure assessed as the proportion of patients with TA-TMA who responded to narsoplimab treatment with a response rate of 61% (17/28) with a 95% CI of (40.6 to 78.5).
L52: Although the Applicant did not identify any new literature sources in this resubmission, after additional consideration, the review team concluded that calculating a response rate based on all patients in the non-pharmacologically NPTP group, including those from studies with less stringent response criteria than Study TMA-001, would likely overestimate an historic response rate and therefore, could be considered as a benchmark for Study TMA-001 to be measured against. The TMA-001 response rate of 61% is higher than the selected literature based historic control response rate 23.3% (95% CI: 15.1, 34.2), with non-overlapping confidence intervals.
L53: The Division concludes that there is a treatment effect of narsoplimab in patients with high-risk TA-TMA based on response rate.
L54: Confirmatory evidence is derived from data for 19 patients from the EAP for which there was patient level data and was discussed in Castelli et al (Castelli et al., 2024). (EAP Subgroup 1). Patients in EAP Subgroup 1 were thrombocytopenic, had evidence of microangiopathic hemolytic anemia, and had at least one feature of high-risk TMA. The overall response rate in EAP Subgroup 1 was 68% (13/19) with a 95% CI of (43.4, 87.4). When examined by age, 9 of 13 adult patients (69%; 95% CI: 38.6, 90.9) and 4 of 6 pediatric patients (67%; 95% CI: 22.3, 95.7) were responders. Thus, the overall response rate and for adults and for the pediatric subsets of EAP Subgroup 1 were consistent with the response rate seen in Study TMA-001.
L55: The Sponsor also submitted analysis and data from a subset of patients enrolled on the Kyoto Stem Cell Transplantation Group registry to serve as an external control for survival endpoints. Due to significant limitations with comparability of the KSTG registry, results cannot be used as comparator for survival data to provide primary evidence or confirmatory evidence for regulatory decision making. See Section 5.3.6.2 for details.
L56: The safety of narsoplimab was primarily assessed in the 28 patients from Study TMA-001. The 221 patients enrolled in the EAP provided supportive safety data. As expected, based on the mechanism of action (MOA) of the drug, narsoplimab has risk of serious infections. In addition, based on the MOA and literature review, there may be a risk of increased susceptibility to tuberculosis. While no patient in clinical trials with narsoplimab were reported to have a positive mycobacterium culture, one patient developed granulomatous orchitis. No information regarding tuberculosis nor granulomatous orchitis will be included in the label because the patient was found to be culture negative (including for mycobacteria) and the infection resolved without treatment. The long-term risk of serious infections will be further assessed in a PMR.
L57: Pediatric and adult TA-TMA share similar underlying pathophysiology with similar characteristics such as endothelial damage and complement activation after HSCT with similar pattern of organ involvement, microangiopathic anemia. In the 6 pediatric patients with subject level data available, the baseline disease characteristics and TMA response rates were consistent with what was observed in the adult population. Safety data is derived from a total of 97 pediatric patients age less than 17 from the EAP. No new safety signals were observed. Given the similar pathophysiology between adults and pediatric patients reasonable to consider partial extrapolation of efficacy from the adult population to support the pediatric indication in pediatric patients age &gt; 2 with TA-TMA.
L58: The proposed dosing regimen allows for an escalation of dosing frequency. Study TMA-001 studied a once weekly regimen, and the EAP employed a twice weekly regimen of narsoplimab. During the review, the Applicant clarified that once weekly administration is appropriate as the starting regimen, however, given inter-patient variability in disease severity and level of lectin
L59: pathway hyperactivation, increasing the frequency to twice weekly administration may be clinically justified in patients who do not respond adequately to narsoplimab treatment. Although direct evidence showing improvement in patient response escalating from once weekly to twice weekly regimen is not available, from a pharmacodynamic (PD) perspective, a more frequent (e.g., twice weekly) dosing regimen is expected to maintain higher lectin pathway inhibition throughout the inter-dosing interval compared to once weekly regimen. It is also reassuring that the safety profile of a twice weekly regimen employed in the EAP is acceptable ensuring no additional safety risk to patients escalating to a more frequent regimen. Although the typical duration of treatment for TA-TMA is relatively shorter (&lt;10 weeks), as patients are frequently monitored, it is still practically feasible to escalate the dosing frequency based on inadequate treatment response.
L60: Narsoplimab addresses an unmet medical need, as this will be the first FDA-approved drug to treat patients with TA-TMA. The benefit-risk profile is favorable for narsoplimab for the treatment of adult and pediatric (age 2 and greater) patients for the treatment of high-risk TMA.
L61: This application resubmission was reviewed by a multidisciplinary review team. The Applicant has adequately addressed all deficiencies in the CRL. Safety review issues were resolved in the original review of the application, see finalized review in DARRTS on October 15, 2021. Each discipline recommends approval, and the signatory authority concurs that the application should be approved. The overall benefit-risk profile is favorable as described in the Benefit-Risk Framework above.
L62: 2. Therapeutic Context
L63: 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L64: TA-TMA occurs when endothelial injury and a complex underlying pathophysiology (Meri S et al., 2022), in the context of an allogeneic hematopoietic cell transplantation, causes microangiopathic anemia and platelet consumption leading to microvascular thrombosis and fibrin deposition. TA-TMA may present with new onset of increasing proteinuria, hypertension, elevated serum creatinine and an increase in transfusion requirements with evidence of intravascular hemolysis. TA-TMA survival rates vary in the reported literature due to different diagnostic criteria, heterogenous populations in terms of severity, index dates (e.g. date of HSCT, date of diagnosis of TMA) and other factors. Some patients will have a self-remitting course but more than one-half of patients will progress to multi-organ failure with mortality rates of greater than 50% at 1 year post-HSCT (Schoettler, 2022) with some literature citing mortality rates for severe TA-TMA exceeding 80% (Jodele et al., 2024, Acosta-Medina et al., 2025, Cho et al., 2008) and reported non-relapse mortality at 42% for patients identified with severe TA-TMA (Vasu et al., 2025).
L65: 2.2. Available Therapy
L66: There are no FDA approved products for the treatment of TA-TMA. The first line of treatment is generally limited to supportive therapy. Initial first line therapy has included withdrawal or minimization of CNIs (i.e. sirolimus, tacrolimus, or both) as these agents have been implicated as risk factors although there is ongoing debate as to whether withdrawal is beneficial. Additional measures include treatment of infections, GVHD and aggressive hypertension management. Off-label medications such as rituximab1, defibrotide2 and complement inhibitors such as C5 inhibitors (ravulizumab3, eculizumab4) have emerged as potential therapeutic agents in TA-TMA.
L67: 1Rituximab is CD20 directed cytolytic antibody indicated for the treatment of adult patients with Non-Hodgkins Lymphoma, adult patients with chronic lymphocytic leukemia, rheumatoid arthritis in combination with methotrexate in adult patients, granulomatosis with polyangiitis and microscopic polyangiitis in adult and pediatric patients 2 years of age and older, moderate to severe pemphigus vulgaris in adult patients.
L68: 2Defibrotide is indicated for the treatment of adult and pediatric patients with hepatic veno-occlusive disease (VOD).
L69: 3Ravulizumab is a complement inhibitor indicated for the treatment of adult and pediatric patients one month of age and older with paroxysmal nocturnal hemoglobinuria (PNH) and atypical hemolytic uremic syndrome.
L70: 4Eculizumab is a complement inhibitor indicated for the treatment of patients with PNH, treatment of patients with aHUS, treatment of generalized myasthenia gravis in adult patients, treatment of neuromyelitis optica spectrum disorder in adult patients.
L71: 3. Regulatory Background
L72: On November 17, 2020, Omeros Corporation submitted an original BLA for narsoplimab for the treatment of patients with hematopoietic stem cell transplant-associated thrombotic microangiopathy (HSCT-TMA). The Applicant received a Complete Response Letter (CRL) on October 15, 2021.
L73: Key regulatory issues discussed with the Applicant in meetings prior to the October 15, 2021, CRL are described in the BLA 761152 clinical review dated October 15, 2021.
L74: Table 1 describes the regulatory meetings with the Sponsor from October 15, 2021, until the submission of the response to the CRL with more detailed summaries in the Appendix (Section 14.5).
L75: Table 1. Summary of Regulatory Meetings from October 15, 2021, to date of current submission

L77: Source: FDA Reviewer
L78: 4. Approach to Review of the Resubmission for Efficacy
L79: In this resubmission, the Applicant included the original efficacy data from Study TMA-001 along with efficacy data (response and survival) for 19 patients treated on the EAP for whom there were patient-level data (EAP Subgroup 1), efficacy data (survival) for 221 patients treated on the EAP (EAP Subgroup 2), and data from 121 patients with TA-TMA who did not receive narsoplimab from the Kyoto Stem Cell Transplantation Group (KSCTG) registry intended to provide a comparator group for the survival endpoints. In addition, the Applicant provided additional data for the 5 responders from the original submission who were assessed by the Agency as non-responders in the original review.
L80: The approach to the review consisted of the following:
L81: 1. Evaluate evidence of benefit based on primary endpoint of response rate:a. Reviewed and assess Sponsor's rationale for the 5 disputed responders in Study TMA-001 and re-adjudicate the Divisions assessment.b. Reviewed the literature based historic response rate.c. Analyzed data for EAP Subgroup 1 for response rate.d. Integrated efficacy analysis data from TMA-001 and EAP Subgroup1 for the primary response endpoint.e. Provided overall summary and conclusions for the primary endpoint.2. Evaluate evidence of benefit based on survival data:a. Analyzed survival data for TMA-001, EAP Subgroup 1, and Pooled (TMA-001 + EAP Subgroup 1), and EAP Subgroup 2.
L82: b. Assessed KSCTG registry and analysis of 1 year survival and Day 100 survival for adequacy as external control for survival endpoints for the TMA-001 and EAP Subgroup 1 populations.c. Provided overall summary and conclusions for the overall survival endpoints.
L83: 5. Evidence of Benefit
L84: 5.1. Sources of Data
L85: 5.1.1. Study TMA-001
L86: Study TMA-001 is a single-arm, open-label study in which 28 patients with TA-TMA received narsoplimab once weekly for a median of 8 weeks (range: 2-16). In total, 24 patients received narsoplimab 4 mg/kg intravenously once weekly and 4 patients received narsoplimab 370 mg intravenously once weekly. Key inclusion criteria were a diagnosis of TA-TMA that persisted at least 2 weeks following modification or discontinuation of treatment with calcineurin inhibitor (CNI) or at least 30 days after transplant with platelet count &lt;150,000/\muL, evidence of microangiopathic hemolysis (presence of schistocytes, serum LDH &gt; upper limit of normal (ULN), or haptoglobin &lt; lower limit of normal), and renal dysfunction.
L87: The primary efficacy endpoint was the proportion of TA-TMA patients who responded to narsoplimab treatment. There were several secondary endpoints to include 100day survival from date of TMA diagnosis and overall survival from the date of TMA diagnosis. For a full list of all the secondary endpoints, refer to the initial review date October 15, 2021 in DARRTS.
L88: Efficacy data from TMA-001 was analyzed for the response (primary) and survival endpoints.
L89: 5.1.2. Compassionate Use Expanded Access Program (EAP)
L90: The single patient expanded access treatment plan (EAP; also known as a compassionate use plan) provides patients access to the investigational product narsoplimab. The purpose of the EAP is to provide access to patients with TA-TMA who are not able to participate in a study and who may benefit from treatment based on the medical judgment of their respective physicians. This is an uncontrolled, case-by-case patient EAP. Dosing for patients \geq 50 kg is narsoplimab 370 mg initially twice weekly and for at least 8 weeks. Dosing for patients \leq 50 kg is 4 mg/kg initially twice weekly and for at least 8 weeks. Change in dose frequency is determined by treatment response.
L91: The treatment course included the following parameters and measurements:
L92: • Terminate treatment after 8 weeks if the patient has not demonstrated improvement in at least platelet count or LDH defined for each as return to pre-TMA baseline level.
L93: • Reduce treatment frequency to once weekly when the patient achieves all of following:o Platelet count return to pre-TMA levelo LDH return to pre-TMA levelo Reduction of platelet and red blood cell transfusions to their respective pre-TMA frequencieso Urine protein/creatinine ratio (uPCR) &lt; 2.0o Resolution of reversible TMA-related neurological abnormalities (e.g., headache, seizure, hallucination, or confusion) or stabilization of irreversible neurological abnormalities (e.g., stroke deficits)o Resolution of TMA-related gastrointestinal signs and symptomso Age-appropriate control of blood pressure on \leq 2 anti-hypertensive medications.• Discontinue treatment when the patient maintains all of the above criteria for 2 weeks while receiving narsoplimab once weekly.• The patient will be retreated according to the original treatment regimen if they experience a relapse of TMA that includes organ dysfunction.
L94: Patient Eligibility
L95: Main Inclusion Criteria
L96: 1. Patient (adult or pediatric) must have undergone allogeneic or autologous hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HSCT).2. Patient, parent or legal guardian must be capable of understanding and complying with the treatment protocol.3. Patient must be capable of understanding an informed consent form (ICF) or the parent or legal guardian of the patient is able to understand and sign a written ICF prior to the initiation of any treatment procedures.4. Patient, parent or legal guardian must voluntarily provide informed consent in accordance with local regulations and governing ethics committee requirements prior to any procedures or evaluations performed specifically for the sole purpose of the program5. If sexually active and of childbearing potential, the patient must agree to practice a highly effective method of birth control throughout treatment with narsoplimab and for at least 12 weeks after the last dose of the drug, such birth-control method defined as one that results in a low failure rate (i.e., less than 1% per year) when used consistently and correctly, such as implants, injectables, combined oral contraceptives, some intrauterine devices, sexual abstinence (abstinence is acceptable when it is in line with the patient's medical condition or preferred and usual lifestyle and is defined as complete abstinence of sexual intercourse, not periodic abstinence or withdrawal), or vasectomized partner.6. Patient must have TA-TMA defined as having thrombocytopenia and evidence of microangiopathic hemolytic anemia.
L97: 7. Based on the physician's judgement, other treatment options or clinical trials in TA-TMA are deemed unsuitable.
L98: Main Exclusion Criteria
L99: 1. Patient has a clinically significant viral, bacterial, or fungal infection not controlled with antimicrobial therapy (prophylactic antimicrobial therapy administered as standard of care is allowed).2. Patient has malignant hypertension (diastolic blood pressure [BP] &gt; 120 mm Hg [for pediatric patients BP &gt; 99th percentile + 5 mm Hg] with bilateral hemorrhages or "cottonwool" exudates on funduscopic examination).3. Patient is pregnant or lactating.4. Patient has a known hypersensitivity to any constituent of the product.5. Presence of any condition that the physician believes would put the patient at risk when receiving narsoplimab.
L100: 5.1.2.1. EAP Subgroup 1 (Castelli et. al.)
L101: The review team identified an article by the Castelli group (Castelli et al., 2024), which describes a retrospective study that took place between January 2018 and August 2023 in Italy. This retrospective study included 20 patients diagnosed with high-risk TA-TMA who received narsoplimab under the EAP compassionate use program and for whom patient level data was available. One patient was included in both Study TMA-001 and this retrospective study. For analysis of the BLA, this patient was analyzed with Study TMA-001 only. This group of patients, referred to as EAP Subgroup 1, comprises 19 patients (13 adult and 6 were pediatric).
L102: The retrospective Castelli study (EAP Subgroup 1) defined high-risk as "the presence of at least one of the following poor prognostic features: (1) rUPCR (urine protein creatinine ratio) \geq 1 mg/mg; (2) the presence of organ dysfunction; (3) LDH levels exceeding two times the ULN; (4) concurrent grade II-IV acute graft-versus-host disease (GvHD); (5) concurrent infections (bacterial or viral)". This is slightly different than the definition in study TMA-001, in which high-risk was defined as having at least one of the following (1) LDH \geq 2x upper limit of normal (ULN), (2) presence of infection, (3) acute GvHD Grade II-IV, and (4) evidence of organ dysfunction. However, a patient with the presence of any one of these features can be considered as having high-risk TA-TMA per Schoettler et al.
L103: For the EAP Subgroup 1, the Applicant provided TMA response results based on two criteria: 1) TMA response using the TMA-001 study response criteria and 2) TMA response as determined by Castelli investigators' opinion regarding whether the patient's TMA signs/symptoms improved/resolved following narsoplimab treatment. The response assessments were identical for the two criteria. For this review, we report the results from analysis based on the criteria used in TMA-001.
L104: The EAP Subgroup 1 was also analyzed for the survival endpoints (overall survival and Day 100 survival) and results described in Section 5.3.3.
L105: 5.1.2.2. EAP Subgroup 2
L106: The Applicant also submitted high level data for all 221 patients treated in the EAP as of March 31, 2025, referred to as EAP Subgroup 2, which included 124 adults and 97 pediatric (defined as &lt; 17 years of age) patients. This group includes the patients in EAP Subgroup 1. Refer to Section 5.1.2 for overall description of the EAP. The EAP subgroup was analyzed for survival data. Refer to Section 4.3 for discussion on survival.
L107: 5.1.3. Kyoto Stem Cell Transplantation Group (KSCTG) registry
L108: With the resubmission, the Sponsor submitted new clinical information which included data from the Kyoto Stem Cell Transplantation Group (KSCTG) registry intended to serve as an external control dataset for Study TMA-001 for survival endpoints. The KSCTG is a multicenter collaboration of transplantation centers across Japan which collects detailed clinical information on patients undergoing allogeneic and autologous hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT). The registry includes patient demographics, disease characteristics, treatment details, and key outcomes such as graft-versus-host disease, relapse, and survival. The KSCTG registry draws its foundational data from Japan's national Transplant Registry Unified Management Program (TRUMP), which systematically collects standardized HSCT data across Japan and captures over 1,000 discrete data elements related to clinical outcomes.
L109: The external control dataset of patients with transplantation-associated thrombotic microangiopathy (TA-TMA) was derived through the following systematic process. KSCTG investigators accessed TRUMP data for 2,915 adult patients who received allogeneic HSCT between January 2000 and September 2016 at 17 KSCTG-affiliated institutions. After applying predefined exclusion criteria, the cohort was refined to 2,425 patients, of whom 134 were flagged by treating physicians as having TA-TMA. Through a secondary survey process involving standardized questionnaires sent to treating physicians and subsequent medical record verification, 121 patients were confirmed to have documented evidence of TA-TMA diagnosis in their medical records and met the predefined diagnostic criteria established by KSCTG investigators, forming the final external control cohort for the comparative analysis. The data flow for the efficacy dataset is shown in
L110: Figure 1

L113: Source: Applicant's response dated May 20, 2025, to FDA information request
L114: The Applicant submitted the KSCTG registry dataset comprising 2,425 HSCT patients, among whom 134 were flagged with “Possible TMA Diagnosis”, and 121 were labeled as “Confirmed TMA Diagnosis”. The Applicant stated that all patients included in the KSCTG TA-TMA database had been confirmed and documented to have TA-TMA by KSCTG investigators before the Applicant became involved with the KSCTG. The Applicant also stated that that they had no role in either the identification of the 134 “possible TMA” patients within the TRUMP registry or in the confirmation process that resulted in the final 121 patients included in the KSCTG TA-TMA database. The external control population comprised the 121 patients with confirmed TA-TMA.
L115: The KSCTG registry data was evaluated for adequacy as an external control for the TMA-001 and the EAP Subgroup 1 survival endpoints. This analysis and interpretation of data is presented in Section 4.3.6.
L116: 5.1.4. Applicant's Summary Report of Literature to Provide External Control for Response for Study TMA-001
L117: In the initial BLA submission, the Applicant proposed a benchmark response rate of 15% in patients with TA-TMA who did not receive narsoplimab to be used as an external control for the primary endpoint. This was based on the Applicant's discussions with clinical experts on TA-TMA. The Applicant's rationale was not acceptable, and FDA requested additional data to justify this 15% response rate to serve as a reference rate for Study TMA-001.
L118: The Applicant submitted their response, which included a summary report1 based on a literature search. The retrospective study population included on HSCT-TMA patients age \geq 18 who had identifiable patient-level data and had data including (i) a response in at least one of the primary response variables used in the TMA-001 study (platelet count, lactate dehydrogenase [LDH], organ function or transfusions) or (ii) report of response AND at least 1 risk factor present in the narsoplimab pivotal trial patients (i.e., GVHD, neurological dysfunction, infection, pulmonary dysfunction, renal dysfunction, gastrointestinal dysfunction, relapse or persistence of underlying malignancy). Excluded from the study were patients who had received eculizumab and/or defibrotide therapy. The report included a description of the demographic and baseline reported risk factors e.g., GVHD, renal dysfunction, neurological dysfunction, along with analysis of the literature-reported frequency of TA-TMA improvement/resolution. This was provided for all patients who met the eligibility criteria ((AP group) and for a subset of patients who had not received any pharmacological treatment (non-pharmacologically treated patients or NPTP group). The report included 35 studies, 25 of which included patients (N=149) in the NPTP group.
L119: Because the literature reports a variety of response criteria, which are not as rigorous as the response endpoint used in Study TMA-001, the Applicant analyzed the criteria according to a definition of stringency i.e., less stringent or equally stringent compared to the TMA-001 criteria, or incomplete. A meta-analysis was performed using a random-effect logistic regression model with the selected studies as the random effect to estimate the overall response rate and the corresponding 95% CI.
L120: The review team identified several significant limitations to the Applicant's literature-based review, including unknown validity and accuracy of data, potential measurement errors associated with endpoints (response rates), propensity score analysis performed on limited confounding variables, timing of treatment visits not standardized across different publications, study conducted using convenience samples and likely selection bias affecting generalizability of the results. Therefore, during the first submission, the review team determined the literature-based review could not be considered sufficient to estimate response rate in an untreated group.
L121: 1 Report on Hematopoietic Stem Cell Transplant-associated Thrombotic Microangiopathy Literature review: Data Extraction, Analysis, and Interpretation; Omeros Corporation, BLA 761152, April 23, 2021.
L122: For the resubmission, the Applicant conducted a new literature review covering publications from 2000-2020 to identify patients with TA-TMA with individual patient-level data and response outcomes, along with an updated search extending from January 2021 to August 2025. However, this new search did not identify any additional studies with necessary patient-level data on TMA response.
L123: For review of this resubmission, the Division re-evaluated the data from the literature and a reasonable historic external control was identified for use as a comparator for response endpoint for Study TMA-001 and EAP Subgroup 1. This is discussed in Section 4.2.1.
L124: 5.2. Analysis of Primary Efficacy Endpoint: TMA Response
L125: The primary efficacy endpoint was the proportion of TA-TMA patients who responded to narsoplimab treatment. Treatment response was defined as both an improvement in TMA-associated laboratory markers (Requirement 1): platelet count and LDH, as well as improvement in clinical status (Requirement 2): either improvement in organ function (renal, pulmonary, GI or neurological) at any time point post-baseline during the study or freedom of transfusion defined as no transfusions (RBC, platelet) for at least 4 weeks from the last transfusion except for patients who died within 4 weeks of the last transfusion. Improvement in organ function was determined by a clinical review of the data by the local site investigators. See the original clinical review in DARRTS dated October 15, 2021, for a detailed description of the study design and statistical analysis plan.
L126: 5.2.1. Literature Review for External Control for Response Rate
L127: To provide an external historic control, the Applicant performed a literature-based study, as described in Section 5.1.4. As previously noted, the Applicant performed analysis on all patients (AP) group and for a subgroup of non-pharmacologically treated patients (NPTP), and within each of these groups an analysis based on comparability to the TMA-001 response criteria (i.e., all criteria, less stringent, equally stringent). These results are provided in Table 19 of the original integrated review for BLA 761152. To remove potential effect of other pharmacological treatments, the Division considered the NPTP group to most closely match the enrolled population in TMA-001.
L128: Using a liberal response definition that identified patients as responders if they met any of the TMA-001 response criteria (rather than requiring improvement in both laboratory markers plus clinical benefit), the response rate in the non-pharmacologically treated patient (NPTP) group was 23.3% (95% CI: 15.1, 34.2). Although an analysis was performed for the subgroup of studies that used a response criterion that was equally stringent as that used in the TMA-001 study, the calculated response rate was higher than in the less stringent group, which would
L129: not be expected. This is most likely due to chance as this subgroup (NPTP-equally stringent) included only 6 studies with a total of 56 patients. The review team chose to use the response rate calculated for the NPTP-all studies, which includes 25 studies and 149 patients, for the comparison historic external control response rate, noting this definition likely overestimates the response rate compared to what would be obtained using the rigorous study criteria.
L130: 5.2.2. Study TMA-001
L131: During the initial review of the application, there were 5 patients whom the Applicant considered responders but whom the review team adjudicated as non-responders. The review team re-analyzed the data for these 5 patients, incorporating additional information for each patient provided by the Applicant in the resubmission. Our original assessment and re-assessment for each of these patients is detailed in Appendix 16.6. The re-assessment incorporated key concepts from a proposed consensus diagnostic criteria and prognostic criteria for TA-TMA published since the initial review (Schoettler et al., 2023). The article defines a group of high-risk TA-TMA patients based on the presence of several poor prognostic factors. All patients (N=28) in Study TMA-001 met at least one of the high-risk criteria for TA-TMA per the proposed consensus diagnostic criteria. See Appendix 14.6 for summary of proposed definition for high-risk TA-TMA.
L132: After careful review, the review team amended its original assessment of response for each of these patients to “responder”. Reasons include:
L133: • Response criteria 1 requirement of demonstration of improvement for both platelets and LDH excluded patients who had not had platelet engraftment as potential responders. For patients who did not have patient engraftment, only LDH response was required to satisfy response criteria 1.• The medical complexity of these patients, including use of agents that potentially confounded evidence of response, necessitates closer review of exposure timelines for narsoplimab and concurrent agents. In the re-assessment, the review team considered for each case, the likelihood of the concurrent drug contributing to the observed response rather than applying an assumption of contribution.
L134: The Division therefore concludes that response was achieved in 17/28 in Study TMA-001 yielding a response rate of 61% with a 95% CI of (40.6, 78.5), the same results the Applicant reported.
L135: 5.2.3. EAP Subgroup 1
L136: TMA Response was assessed in the EAP 1 subset using the same definition as used in Study TMA-001: Improvement in both of two laboratory TMA markers (LDH and platelet counts) and either improvement in organ function or independence from transfusions.
L137: Improvement in platelet count was defined as:
L138: • For baseline platelet count \leq 20,000/\mu\text{L}: (i) \geq 3-fold increase in platelet count, (ii) post-baseline platelet count &gt; 30,000/\mu\text{L}, and (iii) receipt of no platelet transfusions within 2 days prior to the platelet count assessment.
• For baseline platelet count &gt; 20,000/\mu\text{L}: (i) \geq 50\% increase in platelet count, (ii) platelet count &gt; 75,000/\mu\text{L}, and (iii) receipt of no platelet transfusions within 2 days prior to the platelet count assessment.
L139: Improvement in LDH was defined as \text{LDH} &lt; 1.5\times \text{ULN}.
L140: Results for the primary response endpoint overall and by component for the EAP Subgroup 1 are summarized in Table 2. The overall response rate in the EAP Subgroup 1 was 68% (13/19) with a 95% CI of (43.4, 87.4). The point estimates were similar in adults (N=13) with a 69% response rate (95% CI: 38.6, 90.9) and in pediatric (&lt; 17 years of age) patients (N=6) with a 67% response rate (95% CI: 22.3, 95.7).

L145: 5.2.4. Analysis, TMA-001 and EAP Subgroup 1, Response
L146: Although the baseline characteristics, risk stratification factors and TMA response metrics were similar between the EAP Subgroup 1 and the TMA-001 study, there were differences in dosing and use of CNIs limiting a comprehensive integrated analysis for labeling purposes.
L147: 5.2.4.1. Population
L148: As seen in the tables (Table 3 and Table 4), the demographics and baseline disease characteristics are similar between these TMA-001 and the EAP Subgroup 1 patients. In addition, patients in both TA-TMA 001 and the EAP 1 subgroup had at least one feature of high-risk TMA as described by Schoettler et al.

L159: 5.2.4.2. Response Analysis
L160: In Study TMA-001, 17 of 28 patients (61%; 95% CI: 40.6, 78.5) achieved TMA response. The response in EAP Subgroup 1 was 68% (13/19), with 95% CI (43.4, 87.4). When combining adult patients from both studies, 26 of 41 adult patients (63%; 95% CI: 46.9, 77.9) had a response, and the overall combined analysis of all 47 patients showed a TMA response rate of 64% (95% CI: 48.5, 77.3). Detailed results for each population and the pooled total are presented in Table 5.

L164: The response rates from Study TMA-001 (TA-TMA Study) and the response rates for the adult patients in the EAP subgroup 1 are consistent. Due to differences in the dosing of narsoplimab, difference in use of concomitant therapies such as CNIs, between the two groups, no pooled data columns are included in the label.
L165: The Applicant conducted sensitivity analyses using Bayesian methods in response to the Agency's information request (source: Applicant's response dated August 21, 2025).
L166: Their statistical analyses yielded posterior mean estimates for response rates with corresponding 95% credible intervals (CrI) as follows:
L167: 1) TMA-001 + EAP subgroup 1 (N=47) showed a 64% response rate (95% CrI: 50% to 77%);
2) TMA-001 + adult EAP subset (N=41) showed a 63% response rate (95% CrI: 47% to 77%).
L168: In Study TMA-001 and in EAP Subgroup 1, the point estimate and the entire 95% CrI exceeded the conservative literature-based threshold, with posterior probabilities greater than 0.9999. In the EAP Subgroup 1, this was true for both the adult only population and the population overall.
L169: 5.2.5. Conclusions Efficacy: Response Endpoint
L170: During this review, after re-adjudication of the 5 patients whom the review team disagreed with the Applicant's assessment as responders during the review of the initial cycle, the Agency verified the Applicant's primary endpoint response rate of 61% (17/28) with a 95% CI of (40.6 to 78.5) from Study TMA-001.
L171: Although the Applicant did not identify any new literature sources in this resubmission, after additional consideration, the review team concluded that calculating a response rate based on all patients in the NPTP group, including those from studies with less stringent response criteria than Study TMA-001, would likely overestimate an historic response rate, and therefore could
L172: be considered as a benchmark for study TMA-001 to be measured against. The TMA-001 response rate of 61% is higher than the selected literature based historic control response rate 23.3% (15.1, 34.2) with non-overlapping confidence intervals. The Division concludes that there is a treatment effect of narsoplimab in patients with TA-TMA based on response rate.
L173: The response rate in the EAP Subgroup 1 (68% [95% CI: 43.4, 87.4]) is consistent with that observed in Study TMA-001 and is internally consistent for pediatric and adult subgroups.
L174: 5.3. Survival Endpoints
L175: Overall survival (OS) was defined as the time from first dose of narsoplimab to death. Patients who were alive at the time of analysis were censored at the date of last follow-up. Kaplan-Meier (KM) estimates were used to summarize OS for the overall population, as well as for adult and pediatric subgroups. Survival probabilities at 100 days and 1 year were estimated with 95% confidence intervals (CIs) using the log-log transformation.
L176: 5.3.1. Statistical Methods for Evaluating Survival, Study TMA-001 and EAP Subgroups
L177: Overall survival was defined as the time from a specified index date to death, with multiple index dates considered, including time of first dose of narsoplimab, time of TMA diagnosis, and time of HSCT. Survival rates at day 100 and 1 year were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method and the corresponding 95% confidence intervals were calculated using the log-log transformation. Results are presented for all considered index dates.
L178: 5.3.2. Study TMA-001
L179: For the full analysis population in Study TMA-001 (N=28), the estimated survival probability was 73.4% (95% CI: 52.2, 86.4) at day 100 and 41.0% (95% CI: 21.9, 59.2) at 1 year from the date of TMA diagnosis.
L180: Time to event endpoints are difficult to interpret in single-arm trials as lack of a concurrent control group introduces bias, making it hard to isolate the survival effect. Therefore, the survival results from Study TMA-001 are descriptive and were not used to make regulatory decision regarding the treatment effect of narsoplimab on survival.
L181: 5.3.3. EAP Subgroup 1
L182: • Overall population (N=19): The estimated survival probability was 73.7% (95% CI: 47.9, 88.1) at 100 days and 73.7% (95% CI: 47.9, 88.1) at 1 year.
L183: • Adults (N=13): Survival probability at 100 days was 76.9% (95% CI: 44.2, 91.9) and at 1 year 76.9% (95% CI: 44.2, 91.9).
• Pediatric patients (N=6): Survival probability at 100 days was 66.7% (95% CI: 19.5, 90.4) and at 1 year 66.7% (95% CI: 19.5, 90.4).
L184: All the deaths occurred within 100 days of TMA diagnosis. For the Kaplan-Meier Estimates and Curves, please refer to the Appendix, Section 0.
L185: 5.3.4. EAP Subgroup 2
L186: Survival outcome was analyzed for 100 days and 1-year from TA-TMA diagnosis and from first dose of narsoplimab. Results for all patients in the EAP Subgroup 2 and by age group (adult or pediatric) are summarized in Table 6.
L187: The 100-day survival rate from first dose of narsoplimab for all EAP patients was 62.8% (95% CI: 55.3, 69.4) (with a 1-year survival from first dose of 49.6% (95% CI: 41, 57.6)).

L194: Several limitations should be considered when interpreting the survival results in the EAP datasets: the EAP was an uncontrolled observational study with voluntary data reporting, leading to substantial missing data (e.g., risk factors for TA-TMA), while TMA-001 followed rigorous standards; the EAP included heterogeneous populations (both allogeneic and autologous transplants, including patients with prior treatment failures) in different countries with variable clinical practices, whereas Study TMA-001 enrolled only patients with allogeneic HSCT; treatment administration was flexible in the EAP with physician-guided dosing compared to TMA-001's standardized protocol; and Study TMA-001 had enrollment period 2015-2019,
L195: while the EAP spanned 2016 and onward potentially introducing temporal bias from evolving clinical practices and differences in prior or concomitant medications.

L204: 5.3.6. External Control for Survival Endpoints, Kyoto Stem Cell Transplantation Group (KSCTG) registry
L205: For an overview of the KSCTG registry and identification of patients for inclusion in the external control population, see Section 4.3.6
L206: 5.3.6.1. Survival Analysis Plan
L207: The statistical analysis for external control comparison focused on the following:
L208: • comparisons between patients from the pooled dataset of TMA-001 and EAP subset (N=47) to KSTCG confirmed TMA patients (N=121)• comparisons between patients from TMA-001 (N=28) and KSTCG confirmed TMA patients (N=121)
L209: Propensity score-based methods were used to adjust for potential imbalances between cohorts. The propensity score was estimated using logistic regression incorporating the following covariates:
L210: 1) Age (continuous variable)2) Sex (binary: 0 = female, 1 = male)3) Acute GVHD Grade 2-4 (binary: 0 = absent, 1 = present)4) Infection (binary: 0 = absent, 1 = present)5) Neurological dysfunction (binary: 0 = absent, 1 = present)6) Pulmonary dysfunction (binary: 0 = absent, 1 = present)7) Renal dysfunction (binary: 0 = absent, 1 = present)8) LDH \geq 2 times ULN (binary: 0 = no, 1 = yes)9) Donor mismatch (binary: 0 = matched, 1 = mismatched)10) Donor relationship (binary: 0 = unrelated, 1 = related)11) Time from HSCT to TMA diagnosis (discrete variable, days)• In the Applicant's analysis, the propensity score model only contained the first eight covariates listed above, where covariates 3 – 8 are the risk factors for high-risk TMA (Schoettler et al., 2023). In the Agency's analysis, three additional covariates were added in the propensity score model. Donor mismatch and donor relationship were included given their potential association with TA-TMA development. For time from HSCT to TA-TMA diagnosis, substantial differences were observed between cohorts, with treated patients having considerably later onset (TMA-001: mean 120.8 \pm 108.8 days; EAP Subgroup 1: mean 141.6 \pm 120.0 days) compared to KSCTG registry controls (mean 51.5 \pm 58.7 days). These differences may indicate major differences such as disease patterns, severity, or underlying mechanisms. Therefore, time to TA-TMA diagnosis was included as a covariate to address this imbalance.
L211: The Applicant utilized inverse probability of treatment weighting (IPTW) as the primary analytical approach. During the review, extreme propensity score values (approaching 0 and 1) were observed, which can lead to unstable weight estimates and increased variance. To address this concern and evaluate the robustness of treatment effect estimates, several sensitivity analyses were conducted:
L212: • Propensity score matching (PSM): Both 1:1 and 1:2 matching strategies were implemented, with and without caliper restrictions.• IPTW with truncation: Weights were truncated at the 1st and 99th percentiles (1% truncation) and at the 5th and 95th percentiles (5% truncation) to reduce the influence of extreme weights.• Stabilized IPTW: Stabilized weights were calculated to improve the precision of effect estimates while maintaining the benefits of weighting.
L213: These complementary approaches were implemented to evaluate the robustness of the treatment effect estimates and to mitigate bias due to residual covariate imbalance. Survival outcomes were analyzed using Cox proportional hazards regression on the matched dataset for PSM, and weighted Cox regression incorporating the IPTW weights for the weighting analyses. Hazard ratios with corresponding 95% confidence intervals and p-values were reported, and Kaplan–Meier plots were generated to visually display survival distributions between groups.
L214: Overall survival was measured in days from the index date; patients alive at the time of analysis were censored at the last follow-up. For patients from study TMA-001 and EAP Subgroup 1 (Castelli et al.), the index date was defined as the first day of narsoplimab treatment. For patients in the KSTCG registry with TA-TMA, a date of first dose of narsoplimab was not applicable. The index date was therefore imputed as the date of TMA diagnosis plus the median time from TMA diagnosis to first dose of narsoplimab observed in the study TMA-001.
L215: Additional imputation methods were also applied to ensure the robustness of the final results.
L216: 5.3.6.2. Overall Survival Comparison TMA-001 and KSTCG
L217: Results
L218: This section presents the comparison of overall survival between patients from TMA-001 (N=28) and KSTCG confirmed TMA patients (N=121). The distributions of propensity scores are shown in Figure 2. There was low-to-moderate overlap overall, and a lack of common support was observed in parts of the propensity score distribution. Additionally, some propensity scores were at the extremes, which may indicate a potential violation of the positivity assumption. Similar findings were observed in a propensity analysis performed comparing OS between patients in the pooled TMA-001 and EAP Subgroup 1 dataset and KSTCG and can be found in Appendix, Section .

L222: Standardized mean differences (SMDs) were used to assess covariate balance between groups. The SMD quantifies differences in means or proportions between the treated and external control groups relative to the pooled standard deviation. An absolute SMD less than 0.1 is generally considered to indicate adequate balance. Covariate balance plot for the IPTW-based methods is shown below in
L223: Figure 3. While weighting improved balance for many covariates, a number of covariates still had absolute SMDs above the commonly used threshold of 0.1, such as time from HSCT to TMA diagnosis, neurological dysfunction, LDH \geq 2 times ULN and infection, indicating some residual imbalance. These remaining imbalances should be considered when interpreting the adjusted results.

L242: Comparability Issues
L243: There are several limitations with the registry identified by the clinical team, statistical team and the Division of Epidemiology 1 (DEP1) that limit the internal and external validity of the comparative analysis and limits the use of the KSTG registry. Key points from the DEP1-1 consult and the clinical and statistical team are summarized below. A detailed review from DEP1-1 can be found in DARRTS, dated December 15, 2025.
L244: Lack of Patient Comparability
L245: One of the major concerns for the external control study (KSTG registry) is the lack of comparability between the single-arm trial and external control populations, which introduces confounding, selection, and survival bias that may compromise the internal validity of the results.
L246: Temporal Disparities: A significant temporal gap exists between the treatment and external control groups, with the KSCTG control data spanning 2000–2016 while the narsoplimab-treated patients were enrolled from 2015 onward. This temporal disparity is clinically significant because transplant outcomes have improved substantially over this period due to advances in supportive care and infection management. This secular trend is evident within the Applicant's external control data, which demonstrated that the proportion of patients surviving beyond one year increased from 15% (7/48) of KSCTG registry patients in the 2000–2009 period to 25% (18/73) in the 2010–2016 period. This temporal improvement represents a major uncontrolled confounder that biases the results in favor of the narsoplimab group.
L247: • Geographic Differences: The narsoplimab-treated cohorts comprise patients from Study TMA-001, a global study and the EAP Subgroups 1 are derived from patients primarily in Italy (Castelli et al.), while the OS control group consists solely of Japanese registry patients. These geographical variations may introduce potential confounding due to differences in intrinsic or extrinsic factors (ICH E17), e.g. patient demographics, genetic backgrounds,
L248: Narsoplimab-wuug
L249: clinical practices, standard of care, and healthcare systems, thereby limiting the applicability of findings to the US patient population.
L250: • Small Sample Size: The limited number of patients from Study TMA-001 with only 28 patients could limit the generalizability of the findings to a broader TA-TMA population .• Retrospective Analysis: Results of study TMA-001 were already available before the consideration of using the KSCTG registry data as an external control.• Selection Bias and Survival Bias: The single-arm trial's eligibility criteria created a left-truncation effect by requiring patients to survive at least 21-30 days post-transplant to meet diagnostic criteria. This requirement introduced survival bias by systematically excluding patients with early-onset, rapidly progressive disease who would typically be included in a real-world registry. Furthermore, the trial-excluded patients with a life expectancy of less than three months or with certain cardiovascular or hepatic dysfunctions, thereby selecting for a healthier patient population compared to the unselected registry cohort. These selection criteria collectively bias the effect estimate away from the null hypothesis and in favor of the narsoplimab group.• Time from Transplant to TMA Diagnosis: Earlier onset of TMA, which is associated with worse prognosis (Van Benschoten V et al., 2022), was more common in the KSCTG registry. The trial patients excluded those who developed TMA within three weeks following HSCT, while the KSCTG registry include such patients. In the KSCTG registry, 26% (32/121) patients had TMA onset within three weeks, with 6% (2/32) surviving beyond one year, compared to 26% (23/89) among patients with TMA onset beyond three weeks.• Missing Information: Some patient-level data are missing from the registry, such as high-risk factors for TA-TMA, and components to determine TMA response.• Substantial Unmeasured and Residual Confounding: The Applicant utilized a propensity score model to account for potential confounding. However, several important prognostic factors that showed baseline imbalance between the narsoplimab-treated patients and KSCTG controls were excluded from the model. This exclusion led to significant unmeasured confounding that was likely to bias the results in favor of narsoplimab.• Propensity Score (PS) Considerations: The propensity scores demonstrated only low to moderate overlap between the narsoplimab-treated and external control cohorts which could compromise the validity of the comparative analysis. Areas of limited common support and the presence of extreme propensity score values (near 0 or 1) also suggest potential challenges in achieving adequate matching and indicate that some patients may lack comparable counterparts in the opposite group,• Unmeasured Confounding: Registry data may lack detailed information on important prognostic factors (e.g., patients' baseline status, such as LDH and transfusion requirement) that are systematically collected in clinical studies. Additionally, missing covariate information may introduce unmeasured confounding effect. Although the Applicant utilized the E-values to demonstrate that the impact of unmeasurable confounding is not a concern, given the other concerns, such as single registry and selection bias, the exact impact of unmeasurable confounding may not be ruled out.• HLA Mismatch: The proportion of HLA mismatched patients was substantially higher in the KSCTG registry (77%, 93/121) as compared to the trial patients (29%, 8/28). Our analysis of
L251: Narsoplimab-wuug
L252: the KSCTG data revealed that a much lower proportion of HLA-mismatched patients survived beyond one year (17%, 16/93) compared to matched patients (32%, 9/28).
L253: • Stem Cell Source: The use of umbilical cord blood (UCB), which is associated with poorer outcomes (Lim YJ et al., 2022), was far more common in the KSCTG registry (39%, 47/121) than in the narsoplimab groups (2-7%).• Performance Status: In the KSCTG registry, 24% (29/121) of patients had a poor performance score (\geq 2) with 0% surviving beyond one year, compared to a 29% (25/85) of those with better performance status surviving beyond one year.
L254: Additional Methodological and Analytical Concerns
L255: Several technical aspects of the analysis further impact the study's validity and may introduce additional uncertainty.
L256: • Multiple Imputation of Index Date: The study applied simple random sampling approach to impute the index date for KSCTG patients based on the distribution of time interval between HSCT and TMA onset in narsoplimab-treated patients. This indeed assumes that the “latent treatment day” for KSCTG registry patients are “missing” completely at random. This assumption is unlikely to hold given the systematic differences in disease severity, clinical presentation, and healthcare settings between the trial, the EAP, and the registry, where differences in disease severity, patient demographics, healthcare access, clinical presentation may impact treatment timing.• Propensity Score Methodology: In the original submission, the Applicant failed to provide adequate documentation of the propensity score model diagnostics, including assessments of model fit, weight distributions, and covariate overlap. The effectiveness of weight truncation in such a small sample size is also uncertain.• Integration of Heterogeneous Treatment Data: Analysis 2 and 3 of the supplemental study integrated narsoplimab treatment data from all 28 patients in the pivotal trial and all 49 patients in the EAP. However, pooling data from these significantly different sources - a clinical trial versus a real-world setting - without first evaluating for heterogeneity is methodologically inappropriate. This approach risks obscuring the distinct characteristics and outcomes of each patient population.
L257: Although nominal statistical significances were observed in various analyses via different methods, the results should be interpreted with caution considering the aforementioned limitations and comparability issues.
L258: 5.3.7. Conclusion on Efficacy Overall Survival
L259: In the absence of a reliable control arm, there is limited interpretability of the of the time-to-events survival endpoints from Study TMA-001. This remains true even with additional data from EAP Subgroups. Any analysis of EAP subgroup 2 is limited by lack of patient level data, missing data, heterogenous population, and temporal disparity rendering the survival data uninterpretable.
L260: Based on description of the limitations described in the section above, the selection of KSCTG as an external control is problematic and the results are inconclusive. In addition, the registry-based comparator for survival benchmark may introduce the potential for significant potential bias. Therefore, the KSTG registry as a comparator and survival data from the EAP and TMA-001 was not used for primary evidence for regulatory decision making.
L261: The Day 100 survival (from first dose of narsoplimab) for patients from the EAP Subgroup 1 with patient level data and the Day 100 survival for the overall EAP group indicated directional consistency with the efficacy results from Study TMA-001 (Day 100 survival from first dose of narsoplimab). However, due to limitations with interpretation of single-arm survival data, the results can be considered only descriptive and exploratory. Further, as stated in the guideline from American Society for Transplantation and Cellular Therapy, published this year, "Overall survival from TA-TMA diagnosis alone is an insufficient endpoint to understand the impact of TA-TMA directed therapies as patients with TA-TMA may die of multiple etiologies including complications of GVHD, infections, or relapsed disease even after TA-TMA has resolved." (Schoettler et al., 2025).
L262: 5.4. Pediatric Data
L263: The sources of pediatric data for the use of narsoplimab is derived from the 6 patients (age range 5-15) described in EAP 1 group (Castelli et al). In terms of safety, FDA assessed 97 (6 from EAP 1 and 91 from EAP Subgroup 2) pediatric patients (defined as &lt; 17 years of age) from the EAP.
L264: In EAP 1 (Castelli et al) the baseline patient characteristics for the pediatric patients demonstrate that 100% of pediatric patients had an elevated LDH compared to 54% of adults. Both adult and pediatric patients had similar Grade II-IV acute GVHD. The percentage of renal, pulmonary and neurological dysfunction was similar in pediatric and adult patients. The rate of infection in pediatric patients was 100%, and 62% in adults. See Table 3 which presents demographics and baseline disease from 19 patients from EAP 1. The 6 pediatric patients had one criterion that met the high-risk TMA definition as defined by Schoettler et al.
L265: In the EAP 1, pediatric patients had a longer exposure to narsoplimab and, therefore, likely a higher number of doses which was by investigator discretion (see Table 9. Duration of Exposure, Narsoplimab EAP). The Applicant confirms that the longer pediatric treatment duration was due to investigator-directed continuation of therapy after TA-TMA resolution, influenced by relapse prevention considerations.
L266: Efficacy
L267: TMA Response: In the EAP 1 subset (Castelli et al., 2023), 4 of 6 pediatric patients (66.7%, 95% CI: 22.3, 95.7) responded to narsoplimab treatment (see 5. Primary Endpoint, Response Rate, Pooled Analysis). The lower bound of 22.3% reflects the limitation of small sample size (N=6).
L268: The response rate is consistent with the response rate observed in adults, in a high -risk population.
L269: Survival rate at 100 days from first dose of narsoplimab was 69.9% (95% CI: 58.7, 78.7) and at 1 year, 51% (95% CI: 36.6, 63.6) for pediatric patients (N=97) in the EAP2 and the 6 pediatric subjects from EAP 1 (See Table 6. The mortality is high in patients with TA-TMA and 6-month overall survival rate is estimated to be approximately 18% in pediatric population (Jodele et al., 2024). In the pediatric population, Jodele et al. has reported that in high-risk pediatric patients with TA-TMA that did not receive complement inhibitors, OS at 1 year was 16.7% (Jodele et al., 2024). For pediatric patients, 1 year OS in TA-TMA after diagnosis is reported to be 1% in patients treated with therapeutic plasma exchange (Yang et al., 2023). The descriptive survival findings in the pediatric subgroup of EAP 2 and EAP 1 suggest a potential treatment effect, however no definitive conclusions regarding survival can be derived given limitations with survival data from the EAP datasets.
L270: Safety
L271: In the EAP, 97 pediatric patients (defined as &lt; 16 years of age) were exposed to narsoplimab, in the EAP Subgroup 2 and EAP Subgroup 1. The youngest patient exposed to narsoplimab was nine months old. Pediatric patients with TA-TMA being treated with narsoplimab, had 35 (42.2 %) SAEs (see Table 13 in the Appendix). One pediatric patient died. Overall, the safety profile of narsoplimab in pediatric patients with TA-TMA was consistent with the adult patients with TA-TMA exposed to narsoplimab. Labeling should include Warnings and Precautions for serious infections. As with adults, the majority of SAEs were infectious in pediatric patients. The long-term safety of narsoplimab in pediatric patients with TA-TMA will continue to be reviewed through a PMR.
L272: Conclusion
L273: Pediatric and adult TA-TMA share similar underlying pathophysiology with similar characteristics such as endothelial damage and complement activation after HSCT with similar pattern of organ involvement, microangiopathic anemia.
L274: There are differences in pediatric and adult patients in terms of the underlying diseases that necessitated HSCT, where adults usually undergo transplantation for malignant hematologic diseases and pediatric patients more frequently undergo transplantation for non-malignant diseases. Adults are often more likely to have comorbid conditions whereas pediatric patients often do not have baseline comorbidities, however the approach to treatment is similar for pediatric and adult patients with TA-TMA. The course of the disease is similar although there can be differences in baseline comorbidities, underlying disease and concomitant complications such as GVHD which can also impact outcomes.
L275: In the 6 pediatric patients with subject level data available, the baseline disease characteristics and TMA response rates were consistent with what was observed in the adult population. Of
L276: the 97 pediatric patients in the EAP, 89 were 2 years and older and there were no new safety signals were observed. Given the lack of available therapy for this rare disease with high mortality rate and the similar pathophysiology between adults and pediatric patients reasonable to consider partial extrapolation of efficacy and safety from the adult population to support the pediatric indication in pediatric patients age \geq 2 with TA-TMA.
L277: 5.5. Overall Conclusions on Benefit
L278: In Study TMA-001, there is a benefit of estimated TMA response observed in 17 of 28 patients (61%; 95% CI: 40.6, 78.5). No benefit on survival could be confirmed.
L279: 6. Safety Update
L280: A thorough evaluation of safety of narsoplimab in patients with TA-TMA based on data from TMA-001 was performed as part of the review of the original BLA (see finalized review in DARRTS, loaded October 15, 2021). Serious and life-threatening infections have occurred in patients treated with narsoplimab and were reported in 36% (10/28) patients with TA-TMA in clinical trials in patients receiving narsoplimab. These included sepsis, viral infections, pneumonia, bacteremia, fungal infections, gastroenteritis, respiratory tract infections and urosepsis. The most common adverse reactions (incidence &gt;20\%) are viral infections, sepsis, hemorrhage, diarrhea, vomiting, nausea, neutropenia, pyrexia, fatigue and hypokalemia.
L281: Additional safety data from the EAP program was submitted with this resubmission. Analysis of this data did not identify any new or more serious safety signal not already identified the analysis of safety based on the data from TMA-001. Table 9 provides exposure information for the EAP population.
