Plasma protein bindingNarsoplimab-wuug, a monoclonal antibody administered by IV infusion, is expected to be limited to the vascular and interstitial space.

Drug as substrate of transportersNarsoplimab-wuug, a monoclonal antibody, is not expected to be a substrate of transporters.

Elimination

Mass balance resultsNo mass balance study was conducted.

ClearanceMean (%CV): 0.128 (32) L/h

Half-life198 h

Metabolic pathway(s)Narsoplimab-wuug, a monoclonal antibody, is expected to be metabolized into small peptides and amino acids via nonspecific proteolytic degradation.

Primary excretion pathways (% dosage)Elimination of narsoplimab-wuug via renal excretion is expected to be insignificant.

Intrinsic Factors and Specific Populations

Body weightThe clearance of narsoplimab-wuug is affected by body weight; therefore, weight-based dosing of 4 mg/kg is recommended. However, there is a dose cap of 370 mg. A weight-based dosing and dose cap will provide comparable steady-state exposure across all body weight ranges.

AgeAge did not affect narsoplimab-wuug exposure based upon popPK analysis.

Renal impairmentNot applicable as renal excretion of narsoplimab-wuug is expected to be insignificant.

Hepatic impairmentNot applicable as hepatic metabolism is not mechanism for narsoplimab-wuug elimination.
L666: CharacteristicDrug Information

Drug Interaction Liability (drug as perpetrator)



Inhibition/induction of metabolismNarsoplimab-wuug, a monoclonal antibody, is not expected to be an inhibitor or inducer of CYP enzymes.

Inhibition/induction of transporter systemsNarsoplimab-wuug, a monoclonal antibody, is not expected to be an inhibitor or inducer of transporters.

Immunogenicity (if applicable)

BioanalysisAnti-Narsoplimab-wuug antibodies were measured by electrochemiluminescence immunoassay (ECLIA) via C4d on mannan-coated beads.

IncidenceTwo of 6 (33%) healthy subjects who received IV narsoplimab-wuug 4 mg/kg once weekly for 6 weeks developed antidrug antibodies. Narsoplimab-wuug exposure (AUC0-last) was 50% lower (1810 vs. 2781 mcg.h/mL) in the 2 subjects with ADA. In HSCT-TMA patients who received IV narsoplimab-wuug 4 mg/kg or 370 mg once weekly for up to 8 weeks, 2 of 28 (7%) developed antidrug antibodies. One of the 2 HSCT-TMA patients developed neutralizing antibodies.

Clinical impactCompared to HSCT-TMA patients without antidrug antibodies, total clearance (CLtotal) was greater in HSCT-TMA patients with antidrug antibodies. Based upon popPK Vmax was 143% higher in patients with ADA, and is predicted to result in 22% lower AUCss and 52% lower Cmin,ss. The patient who developed neutralizing antibody was a treatment responder.
L667: Abbreviations: ADA, antidrug antibody; CV, coefficient of variation; ELISA, enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay; HRP, horseradish peroxidase; HSCT-TMA, hematopoietic stem cell transplant-associated thrombotic microangiopathy; IV, intravenous(ly); mAb, monoclonal antibody; MASP-2, mannan-binding lectin-associated serine protease; PK, pharmacokinetic; popPK, population pharmacokinetics; TMB, 3,3',5,5'-tetramethylbenzidine
L668: 5.1. Nonclinical Assessment of Potential Effectiveness
L669: Summary: Narsoplimab is specific for MASP-2 and selectively blocks lectin-initiated complement activation. The lectin pathway contributes to complement (C) activation and thrombus formation in defined in vitro and in vivo models of endothelial injury, which presumably reflect key elements of TMAs. Narsoplimab inhibits this contribution, but other C-activation pathways are also contributory; thus, vascular occlusion appears delayed, but occlusion still occurred in the murine model of TMA.
L670: Background: OMS721 (narsoplimab) binds MASP-2 with a K_D of 62-73pM and selectively inhibits the lectin pathway of complement (IC_{50} 2.5nM). Specificity for the lectin pathway was evaluated by demonstrating a dose-dependent inhibition of lectin-dependent membrane attack complex (MAC) deposition with an IC_{50} of 1nM without significant inhibition of the alternative- or classical pathway-induced MAC deposition up to 100nM. In vivo studies in healthy animals demonstrated that OMS721 has a pharmacokinetic-pharmacodynamic (PK-PD) relationship with eventual saturation of lectin-dependent C4 and C3 activation.
L671: Key pharmacodynamic effects of OMS721 were evaluated in an ex vivo model of aHUS and an in vivo mouse model of TMA. In the ex vivo aHUS study, serum from patients with aHUS (during the acute phase of the disease and at remission) was incubated with activated human microvascular endothelial cells in vitro in the presence and absence of OMS721.
L672: OMS721 partially reduced C5b-9 deposition (range 40% - 54%; Figure 1) and reduced platelet adherence (range 57% - 79%; Figure 2) on the surface of microvascular endothelial cells at the timepoint measured upon exposure to serum of patients with aHUS both in the acute phase and in remission. Of note, Figure 2 indicates that the pan-complement inhibitor sCR1 abrogated C5b-9 deposition whereas narsoplimab exhibited partial inhibition, demonstrating that complement pathways other than lectin substantially contribute to C5b-9 deposition induced by aHUS serum.

L679: In vivo efficacy of OMS721 was assessed in a mouse model of TMA in which localized thrombosis was induced by photo-activation of fluorescein isothiocyanate-labeled dextran in the microvasculature of the cremaster muscle of C57Bl/6 mice.
L680: Administering OMS721 one hour before induction of thrombosis delayed the initiation of thrombus formation and the time to complete vessel occlusion, demonstrating a contribution of the lectin complement pathway to the thrombotic response to injury (Figure 3). However, over time, thrombus formation still occurred in a similar percentage of mice regardless of treatment, suggesting that pathways in addition to lectin C activation contribute to and eventually result in microvascular thrombosis.
L681: Figure 3. OMS721 Delays Onset of Thrombus Formation (top panel) and Microvascular Occlusion (bottom panel) in the Mouse Model of FITC-Dextran-Induced TMA

L688: 6. Assessment of Effectiveness
L689: 6.1. Dose and Dose Responsiveness
L690: 6.1.1. Study TMA-001
L691: The proposed narsoplimab-wuug dose and dosing regimen is 4 mg/kg, given as a 30-minute IV infusion (up to 370 mg per infusion) once weekly. The clinical studies to support the proposed dose and dosing regimen include a multiple, dose-escalation study (OMS721-NHV-002) in healthy subjects and a small safety and efficacy study (TMA-001) in HSCT-TMA patients.
L692: The Applicant conducted a multiple dose escalation study (OMS721-NHV-002) in healthy male and female subjects to assess the PK, PD, and safety of narsoplimab-wuug IV and SC. The study included assessment of C4d inhibition, a pharmacodynamic marker of the lectin pathway that the Applicant hypothesized would be representative of clinical efficacy. After the first IV infusion of narsoplimab-wuug 4 mg/kg, maximum inhibition of C4d was approximately 95% at 6 hours after the start of infusion and was sustained above 80% from 48 to 312 h after the last dose. With narsoplimab-wuug 2 mg/kg, C4d inhibition above 80% was maintained from 2 to 48 h after the first dose. The ex-vivo assay of C4d inhibition was used to justify the selection of a maximum dose of 4 mg/kg IV dosing in the Phase 2 studies as it was expected to maintain mean lectin pathway inhibition &gt;80% for about 168 hours, the proposed dosing interval.
L693: Evaluation of inhibition by narsoplimab-wuug of the lectin-pathway of complement activation, and specifically the lectin-mediated complement component 4d (C4d) formation, is not an unreasonable approach to dose selection; however, it is important to note that there are no available biomarkers known to predict treatment response in HSCT-TMA.
L694: The Applicant conducted Study TMA-001, which was initially a proof-of-concept, dose-finding study in patients with HSCT-TMA, but then was submitted as the ‘pivotal’ safety and efficacy study for the proposed indication. In stage 1 of Study TMA-001, narsoplimab-wuug was administered to patients in 3 cohorts (Cohorts 1, 2, and 3), with dose escalation by cohort (0.67 mg/kg, 2 mg/kg, and 4 mg/kg, respectively) in order to identify the optimal dosing regimen. After enrollment and dosing at each dose level, a safety review by the clinical study monitoring team was conducted to determine whether dose escalation should proceed. In Stage 2 of the Core Study (refers to data collected under protocol versions prior to Amendment 11.1), the dose selected in the first stage (4 mg/kg) was administered to patients by disease etiology (aHUS alone in Cohort 4; TTP or HSCT-TMA in Cohort 5). Patients enrolled in Stages 2 and 3 prior to Amendment 10 (Appendix III.15) received narsoplimab-wuug 4 mg/kg. As of Amendment 10 of the protocol, newly enrolled patients received a fixed narsoplimab-wuug dose of 370 mg. Preliminary PK evaluation suggested that clearance (CL) was not affected by body weight. In the Core Study, patients in Stage 2 could proceed to Stage 3 based on the patients’ response to therapy, safety, and tolerability.
L695: The review team’s assessment found no association between narsoplimab-wuug exposure or trough concentrations or the extent of C4d inhibition and the response to narsoplimab-wuug treatment in patients with HSCT-TMA. Almost all non-responders to narsoplimab-wuug treatment had consistently comparable or higher exposure than patients who responded to
L696: treatment, with trough narsoplimab-wuug concentrations consistently above IC80 for C4d inhibition in majority of non-responders (Figure 34, Section 14.3.4). Conversely, amongst the treatment responders, many patients had trough narsoplimab-wuug concentrations just above IC50 for C4d inhibition.
L697: Therefore, although related to the mechanism of action of the drug, the extent of C4d inhibition does not appear to be predictive of treatment response in HSCT-TMA patients at the studied dosage.
L698: 6.2. Clinical Trials Intended to Demonstrate Efficacy
L699: 6.2.1. Study OMS-721-TMA-001 (TMA-001)
L700: Overview and Objectives
L701: To support the proposed indication, the Applicant conducted Study TMA-001, titled “A Phase 2, Uncontrolled, Three-Stage, Dose-Escalation Cohort Study to Evaluate the Safety, Pharmacokinetics, Pharmacodynamics, Immunogenicity, and Clinical Activity of OMS721 in Adults with Thrombotic Microangiopathies.”
L702: Study TMA-001 was originally designed to provide preliminary information on the safety and efficacy of narsoplimab-wuug for the treatment of TMAs (aHUS, TTP, or HSCT-TMA). Based on preliminary results, the study was modified to support the indication for treatment of HSCT-TMA with narsoplimab-wuug.
L703: Primary Objective (HSCT-TMA):
L704: The copimary objectives of this study were to:
L705: • Assess the safety and tolerability of multiple-dose administration of narsoplimab-wuug in patients with TMA, including patients with HSCT-TMA, aHUS, and TTP• Evaluate the efficacy of narsoplimab-wuug in patients with HSCT-TMA by response. Response is defined as meeting both of the following criteria:— Improvement in TMA laboratory markers of platelet count and LDH— Improvement in clinical status (explained in detail below)
L706: Secondary Objectives (HSCT-TMA):
L707: • 100-day survival• Overall survival• Platelet count change from baseline• Lactate Dehydrogenase (LDH) change from baseline• Haptoglobin change from baseline
L708: • Hemoglobin change from baseline• Duration of response (defined as the time between the first date a response is achieved and the first date the platelet count decreases by more than 50% from the maximum and LDH increases to &gt;150% of the upper limit of normal).• Creatinine change from baseline in patients who had no reason other than HSCT-TMA for creatinine elevation• Freedom from platelet transfusion in patients who received at least 1 platelet transfusion within 2 weeks of the first narsoplimab-wuug dose• Freedom from red blood cell (RBC) transfusion in patients who received at least 1 RBC transfusion within 2 weeks of the first narsoplimab-wuug dose• The PK of multiple-dose administration of narsoplimab-wuug (this will be assessed in patients with HSCT-TMA as well as patients with aHUS and TTP)• The PD of multiple-dose administration of narsoplimab-wuug (this will be assessed in patients with HSCT-TMA as well as patients with aHUS and TTP)• The immunogenicity of multiple-dose administration of narsoplimab-wuug (this will be assessed in patients with HSCT-TMA as well as patients with aHUS and TTP)
L709: Study Design
L710: Study TMA-001) is a Phase 2, open-label, uncontrolled, 3-stage ascending-dose-escalation study in patients with TMA, specifically aHUS, TTP, and HSCT-TMA. The study design described below will focus on HSCT-TMA patients which is used to support the HSCT-TMA indication for narsoplimab-wuug.
L711: In Stage 1 of the study, narsoplimab-wuug was administered to 3 cohorts (Cohort 1, Cohort 2, and Cohort 3), with dose escalation by cohorts. Each patient in stage 1 received 4 weekly doses of narsoplimab-wuug. In Stage 2 of the study, the dose selected in the first stage (4 mg/kg IV) was administered to expanded cohorts. Prior to Amendment 10, patients in Stage 2 with HSCT-TMA received 4 weekly doses (See Table 115 for details on protocol amendments). Patients in Stage 2 who demonstrated improvement in TMA signs during their initial treatment period were eligible for Stage 3, which provided an additional 4 weekly doses in patients with HSCT-TMA. Dosing and administration of narsoplimab-wuug is described in Table 6. Design schema of Stage 1, 2, and 3 (HSCT-TMA patients only) are shown in Figures 4, 5, and 6 below. Standard treatments were continued on study.

L727: Source: Applicant's TMA-001 protocol
L728: The protocol for Study TMA-001 underwent 12 amendments;, description of all amendments is listed in Table 112. Amendment 10 discontinued enrollment of patients with aHUS and TTP and changed the dose of narsoplimab-wuug from 4 mg/kg IV to a fixed dose of 370 mg IV for patients with HSCT-TMA. Enrollment was closed for aHUS patients because a Phase 3 study was opened for aHUS patients (Study OMS721-HUS-002). Enrollment was closed for TTP patients because there was no apparent benefit of narsoplimab-wuug over standard of care. Amendment 11.1 provided for retrospective collection of additional data from patients with HSCT-TMA, including changes to the primary and secondary objectives to focus on HSCT-TMA, and defined criteria by which to determine clinical response to treatment. As of
L729: amendment 11.1, 28 subjects had been enrolled. The additional data collected included patient demographics, baseline conditions, donor characteristics, the transplant procedure, concomitant medications, TMA-related laboratory measures, transfusions, transplant complications, and outcomes. Data were collected from medical records from the time of transplant conditioning through the last available patient contact with the site or up to 2 years (104 weeks) after the first narsoplimab-wuug dose, whichever was earlier.
L730: Data collected under protocol versions prior to Amendment 11.1 (the Core Study) are referred to as Core Data. Collection of the additional data defined in Amendment 11.1 is referred to as additional data collection (ADC). Not all data were available in the ADC due to the retrospective collection from medical records. Clinical sites or third-party vendors unaffiliated with Omeros then reviewed the site medical record of each patient and entered the data as available.
L731: Reviewer Comment
L732: The retrospective data collection of additional data from patients with HST-TMA occurred after all 28 patients were enrolled. Ideally, response criteria should be pre-defined.
L733: 6.2.1.1. Key Inclusion and Exclusion Criteria for HSCT-TMA Patients
L734: Inclusion Criteria
L735: • Age \geq 18 years at screening• Patients with persistent HSCT-TMA were defined as having all of the following at least 2 weeks following modification or discontinuation of calcineurin inhibitor treatment or at least 30 days after the transplant:— Platelet count &lt; 150,000/\mu\text{L}— Evidence of microangiopathic hemolysis (presence of schistocytes, serum LDH &gt; upper limit of normal (ULN), or haptoglobin &lt; LLN)— Renal dysfunction (doubling of serum creatinine from pretransplant)• No clinically apparent alternative explanation for thrombocytopenia and anemia.• If sexually active and of childbearing potential, must have agreed to practice a highly effective method of birth control until the end of the study
L736: Exclusion Criteria
L737: • Had eculizumab therapy within 3 months prior to screening• Had Shiga toxin-producing Escherichia coli-induced HUS• Had a positive direct Coombs test• Had an active systemic bacterial or fungal infection requiring antimicrobial therapy (prophylactic antimicrobial therapy administered as standard of care was allowed)
L738: • Had a poor prognosis with a life expectancy of less than 3 months in the opinion of the Investigator• Were pregnant or lactating• Had received treatment with an investigational drug or device within 4 weeks prior to screening• Had abnormal liver function tests defined as alanine aminotransferase (ALT) or aspartate aminotransferase (AST) &gt;5 times ULN
L739: Clinical reviewer comment
L740: The Applicant also submitted patient narratives to better characterize the HSCT-TMA population. The inclusion and exclusion criteria define a HSCT-TMA patient population with evaluable endpoints.
L741: 6.2.1.2. Study Endpoints
L742: Primary Endpoint
L743: The primary efficacy endpoint was the proportion of HSCT-TMA patients who responded to narsoplimab-wuug treatment. Treatment response was defined as a patient with HSCT-TMA who demonstrated both an improvement in laboratory TMA markers (Requirement 1; platelet count and LDH) as well as improvement in clinical status (Requirement 2; either improvement in organ function or freedom from transfusion) at any time point post baseline during the study.
L744: Requirement 1: Improvement in laboratory TMA markers
L745: • Improvement in platelet count— For patients with baseline platelet count \leq 20,000/\mu\text{L}: tripling of baseline platelet count AND postbaseline platelet count &gt; 30,000/\mu\text{L} AND no platelet transfusions 2 days before and on the day of the platelet count collection— For patients with baseline platelet count &gt; 20,000/\mu\text{L}: increase in platelet count \geq 50\% AND postbaseline platelet count &gt; 75,000/\mu\text{L} AND no platelet transfusions 2 days before and on the day of the platelet count collection• Improvement in LDH is defined as LDH &lt; 1.5 times ULN
L746: Requirement 2: Improvement in clinical status OR freedom from transfusion
L747: • Improvement in organ function— Improvement in renal function as evidenced by any of the following: 1) a reduction of creatinine &gt; 40\%; 2) creatinine &lt; \text{ULN} and reduction of creatinine &gt; 20\%; or 3) discontinuation of renal replacement therapy (RRT)— Improvement in pulmonary function as evidenced by either of the following criteria: 1) extubation and discontinuation of ventilator support; or 2) discontinuation of noninvasive mechanical ventilation (continuous positive pressure ventilation).
L748: — Improvement in neurological function as evidenced by either of the following criteria:1) improvement in reversible neurological conditions (e.g., cessation of seizures); or2) stabilization of irreversible neurological conditions (e.g., stability of neurological deficits following stroke without further deterioration or subsequent strokes). The neurological deficits considered for improvement were limited to stroke, seizures, posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome (PRES), and weakness.— Improvement in gastrointestinal function (gastrointestinal HSCT-TMA requires diagnosis by tissue biopsy) as measured by improvement in the gastrointestinal staging in the Mount Sinai Acute GVHD International Consortium (MAGIC) criteria• Freedom from transfusion defined as no transfusions for at least 4 weeks from the last transfusion
L749: Secondary Endpoints
L750: • 100-day survival, from the date of TMA diagnosis• Overall survival, from the date of TMA diagnosis• Freedom from platelet transfusion defined as no platelet transfusions for at least 4 weeks following the last platelet transfusion except for patients who died within 4 weeks of the last platelet transfusion (only evaluated in patients who had platelet transfusions in the 2 weeks prior to or on the first narsoplimab-wuug dose date)• Freedom from RBC transfusion defined as no RBC transfusions for at least 4 weeks following the last RBC transfusion except for patients who died within 4 weeks of the last RBC transfusion (only evaluated in patients who had RBC transfusions in the• 2 weeks prior to or on the first narsoplimab-wuug dose date)• Change from baseline in platelet count (postbaseline platelet counts without platelet transfusions 2 days before and on the day of the platelet count collection were used) over time using central laboratory values• Change from baseline in LDH over time using central laboratory values• Change from baseline in haptoglobin over time using central laboratory values• Change from baseline in hemoglobin (postbaseline hemoglobin without RBC and whole blood transfusions 6 days before and on the day of the hemoglobin collection were used) over time using central laboratory values• Change from baseline in creatinine over time in patients who had no reason other than HSCT-TMA for creatinine elevation using central laboratory values• Duration of response, defined as the number of days from the first response date to the first relapse date
L751: Clinical reviewer comment
L752: A Type A meeting with the Applicant occurred on May 2, 2019, in which endpoints were discussed. The Agency and Applicant reached agreement on response based on LDH and
L753: transfusion freedom. The Agency pointed out concerns regarding interpretation of magnitude of platelet improvement and renal improvement. These components were later agreed to after correspondence with the Applicant in written communication on July 9, 2019. The Agency commented that there may be some overlap (i.e., presence of both TMA and GVHD) and that the Applicant will need to address this in narratives and discussions for each patient. In a Type A meeting on January 6, 2020 the Agency stated the evaluation of weakness is difficult in patients with multiple comorbidities and who require intensive care treatment. There may be multiple causes for a patient's weakness therefore the Agency did not agree with the assessment of weakness to assess neurological improvement. In addition, The Agency stated improvement in confusion, agitation, headache will be difficult to assess given multitude of contributing factors to these signs and symptoms.
L754: Statistical Analysis Plan
L755: Sample Size Determination
L756: The sample size for each cohort (aHUS, and HSCT TMA/TTP) was determined by comparison to the total sample size of uncontrolled Phase 2 studies that provided sufficient data for approval of eculizumab for the treatment of aHUS. The Applicant stated that 28 patients are adequate because of the orphan disease status. The Applicant stated that it was expected that approximately 28 HSCT-TMA patients enrolled with observed response rate of 50%, results in the exact 95% CI 30.6%, 69.4%.
L757: Reviewer Comment
L758: There was no pre-agreement between the Agency and the Applicant that 28 patients was an adequate sample size.
L759: Control
L760: Study TMA-001 is a single arm study, neither concurrent control nor historical control was utilized.
L761: Reviewer comment
L762: During the development of narsoplimab-wuug the possibility of establishing a historical control was discussed, and the Agency pointed out potential barriers with this approach. A single-arm trial based on response endpoint could possibly provide sufficient magnitude of treatment effect and be acceptable to support the biologics license application (BLA) submission given the rarity of HSCT-TMA and the high risk for poor outcomes in a population with no approved therapies. The Agency also agreed with the modification of the primary endpoint in Amendment 11.1 in order to assess response in patients with HSCT-TMA and the additional data collection.
L763: Randomization
L764: This was a single arm trial with no randomization required.
L765: Interim Analysis
L766: No interim analysis was planned.
L767: Missing Data
L768: Non-responder imputation was used to handle missing data for the binary efficacy endpoints. Time-weighted average analysis and repeated measure model was used to analyze the continuous secondary efficacy endpoints over time.
L769: The TMA diagnosis date must have had at least the month and the year. If the day was missing, the day was be imputed as 1.
L770: For patients whose death dates were incomplete, if only the day of the death date was missing, the day was imputed as the first day of the month. If either the month or the year of the death date was missing, the death date was not imputed, and the overall survival was censored at the last known date of alive in the database. For patients who were alive, overall survival was censored at the last known date of alive in the database.
L771: Primary Efficacy Analysis
L772: Efficacy analyses were performed for the full analysis set (FAS) population and the Per-protocol Patient Set (PPS) population. The FAS population included all HSCT-TMA patients who received any amount of study drug. The PPS population included all HSCT-TMA patients who received at least 4 weeks of study treatment (treatment period was greater than or equal to 28 days).
L773: Analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint used a non-responder imputation to impute missing or incomplete data. The number and percent of responders were summarized with an exact 95% CI (Clopper-Pearson) by dose and total for the FAS and PPS populations. If the lower bound of the exact 95% CI was greater than 15%, the sponsor determined that there was sufficient evidence to demonstrate efficacy of narsoplimab-wuug in the target patient population.
L774: Statistical reviewer comment
L775: In the original BLA submission, the Applicant did not provide an adequate justification for the chosen 15% response rate in patients with HSCT-TMA. The Agency recommended that the Applicant conduct a literature search to justify 15% response rate (see Section 6.2.2 below).
L776: Subgroup Analysis
L777: • Subgroup analyses for the following subgroups was performed for the FAS population:— Age (&lt;65, ≥65 years)— Gender (Male or Female)— GVHD (Yes or No)— Significant infection (Yes or No)
L778: — Graft failure or non-engraftment (Yes or No)— Female-to-male transplant (Yes or No)— Multiple organ TMA involvement (Yes or No)— Mismatched donor (Yes or No)— Baseline platelet counts (&lt;20,000 or \geq 20,000 platelets/\mu\text{L})— Renal dysfunction at baseline (Creatinine &gt; ULN or spot urine protein \geq 2+ or &gt;30 mg/dL; Yes or No)— Any transfusions within the 2 weeks prior to or on the first OMS721 dose date (Yes or No)• A completer analysis was performed using the patients with non-missing responses only. The exact 95% CI for the completer response rate was provided for the FAS population by dose.• Two alternative definitions of response were analyzed:— The first alternative response was defined as achieving an improvement in TMA markers and an improvement in renal function. The number and percent of the patients achieving the first alternative response was summarized with an exact 95% CI by dose for the FAS population.— The second alternative response was defined as the same as the primary endpoint by excluding the neurological function. The number and percent of the patients achieving the second alternative response were summarized with an exact 95% CI by dose for the FAS population.
L779: Secondary Efficacy Analyses
L780: The following secondary efficacy endpoints were proposed:
L781: • 100-day Survival: The 100-day survival status was determined as a binary variable. A patient achieved a 100-day survival if the overall survival from the date of HSCT-TMA diagnosis of the patient would have been greater than or equal to 100 days. If the 100-day survival status of a patient was unknown (i.e., censored before 100 days), this patient would have been considered to have died prior to 100 days. The 100-day survival rate was estimated with an exact 95% CI by dose for the FAS population.• Overall Survival: Overall survival was calculated from the date of HSCT-TMA diagnosis. For patients who were alive, their overall survival was censored at the last known date of alive in the database. Overall survival was analyzed using Kaplan Meier method by dose for the FAS population. Median overall survival and its 95% CI was estimated by dose.• Duration of Response: Duration of response was defined as the number of days from the first response date to the first relapse date (first relapse date – first response date +1). Duration of response was censored at the last platelet or LDH collection date if relapse did not occur during the study. Kaplan-Meier analysis of the duration of response was
L782: performed for the patients who achieved a response by dose for the FAS population.Median duration of response was presented with 95% CI.
L783: Patient Disposition
L784: Disposition data for patients with HSCT-TMA are provided in Table 7. A total of 8 patients (28.6%) discontinued study drug. In the Core Study, 15 patients (53.6%) discontinued the study, and 13 (46.4%) patients completed the study. All 13 patients responded to study drug. Three patients withdrew for nonstudy-related reasons (“Other” in Table 7) following treatment completion and each responded to study drug. Ten patients (35.7%) remained alive through the last site contact for ADC. No patients remained on treatment.

L791: Demographic Characteristic and Baseline Characteristics
L792: Demographics summary and some baseline characteristics of patients are provided in Table 8. Most patients were male (20 patients, 71%) and white (17 patients, 61%). There were no marked
L793: differences in baseline weight or body mass index between patients who received a fixed dose of 370 mg and those who received the target weight-based dose of 4 mg/kg although all patients receiving the fixed dose were male.
L794: Median time to TMA diagnosis after transplant was 73.5 days (range: 21 to 436 days). Twenty-five patients (89%) received transfusions within 2 weeks prior to treatment with narsoplimab-wuug. Five patients (18%) received prior plasma therapy.

L806: Efficacy Results-Primary Endpoint
L807: In the FAS, the Applicant’s results showed that 17 of 28 patients with HSCT-TMA (61%) responded to narsoplimab-wuug treatment (Table 10), and the 95% CI for response was 40.6% to 78.5%. This response rate was statistically superior to the protocol-specified response rate in this high-risk population of 15% (p &lt; 0.0001). As a reminder the PPS population include all HSCT-TMA patients who received at least 4 weeks of study treatment (treatment period is greater than or equal to 28 days). The Applicant’s results based on PPS appear to support their findings based on FAS population.
L808: Within this overall composite response endpoint, the Applicant’s results suggest that each of the 3 contributing clinical measures showed improvement in the FAS: 17 of 28 patients (61%) showed improvement in both platelet count and LDH. However, FDA’s evaluation showed that 12 of 28 patients (43%) had improvement in both platelet count and LDH; 20 of 27 patients (74%) had improvement in at least one organ function; 12 of 25 patients (48%) experienced freedom from RBC and/or platelet transfusion after narsoplimab-wuug treatment (see Table 10). The clinical team could not conclude a treatment effect for the overall composite endpoint nor for the individual parameters below, see Section 6.3.1 for further details.

L818: A detailed summary of the Applicant's results for improvement in platelet count and LDH in patients treated with narsoplimab-wuug is provided in Table 11 for both FAS and PPS. In particular, improvement in platelet count was assessed in patients relative to their baseline platelet count. The Applicant's results demonstrated that in patients with very low baseline platelet counts (20,000 or lower), 3 of 6 patients (50%) showed improvement (defined as a platelet count at any time point that was greater than 30,000 and at least 3-fold above baseline platelet levels); In patients with baseline platelet counts over 20,000, 11 out of 17 patients (65%) showed improvement (defined as a platelet count at any time point that was greater than 75,000 and 1.5-fold or more above baseline platelet levels). Twenty-one (75%) of the patients showed improvement in LDH (defined as an LDH &lt;1.5 times the ULN). There was an overall improvement in TMA markers in 17 patients (61%).

L827: Reviewer Comment
L828: The protocol stated that if a patient is not evaluable for a response criterion due to confounding factors, the non-evaluable response criterion will not be included in the determination of responder. Evaluability of each response criterion will be determined by a clinical review of relevant data. The review team did not agree with this allowance which is further discussed in Section 6.3.1.
L829: A detailed summary of the Applicant’s results for improvement in organ function in all patients (FAS) with HSCT- TMA and in the PPS is provided in Table 12. As seen from the table, the Applicant reported that twenty of 27 patients (74%) experienced improvement in at least 1 organ function in the FAS with the majority evaluated on the basis of renal function (27/28 patients, 96.4%). Eighteen of the 27 patients (67%) showed improvement in renal function, with 10 patients (37%) showing a reduction of creatinine levels of greater than 40%, and 15 patients (56%) recorded creatinine levels less than the ULN and reduction of creatinine &gt; 20\%. Of the 6 patients with neurologic dysfunction at baseline, 3 patients (50%) showed improvement in evaluable neurologic function. These improvements were all in reversible neurological conditions, and there was no stabilization of irreversible conditions. Improvements in organ function in the PPS were generally similar to those in the FAS, with 17 of 22 patients (77%) experiencing an improvement in organ function.

L845: The Applicant’s results showed that there were 25 patients in the FAS who had required an RBC or platelet transfusion in the 2 weeks leading up to study entry. Of these patients, 12 (48%) experienced freedom from transfusion for a consecutive 4-week period after narsoplimab-wuug treatment. The patients showing freedom from transfusion were made up of 8 of 18 patients (44%) who had required platelet transfusion and 11 of 22 (50%) patients who had required RBC transfusion prior to narsoplimab-wuug treatment. Results in the PPS were generally similar to those in the FAS, with 11 of 20 patients (55%) experiencing freedom from transfusion.

L854: Sensitivity Analyses
L855: No response status was missing. Therefore, the analysis with non-missing responses was identical to the primary analysis. For TMA markers and renal function only, the FDA’s analysis results showed that 12 of 28 (43% with 95% CI, 24.5% to 62.8%) patients in the FAS responded to treatment.
L856: Efficacy Results-Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
L857: 100-Day and Overall Survival
L858: A summary of 100-day survival and overall survival (OS) following narsoplimab-wuug treatment in the FAS, PPS, and treatment responders from the FAS is shown in Table 14. Survival days were calculated from the day of TMA diagnosis. At Day 100 after HSCT-TMA diagnosis, 19 patients (68%) remained alive in the FAS, and the median OS was 274 days. For the PPS, the 100-day survival rate was 83%, and the median OS was 361 days. Among patients who responded to narsoplimab-wuug treatment, 100-day survival rate was 94% while OS was not estimable.

L878: Clinical reviewer comment
L879: The definition of 100-day survival was defined as survival 100 days after diagnosis of HSCT-TMA and the definition of overall survival was survival after diagnosis of HSCT-TMA. In the transplant setting there are different definitions of survival as well as different definitions used in the literature. In the transplantation setting, 100-day survival is often defined as survival 100 days after the transplantation date (Day 0) and overall survival is defined as survival after date of transplantation. Therefore, it is difficult to interpret any survival data due to differences in definitions and lack of adequate control.

L881: Table 15 provides a summary of durability (no relapse) and duration of response for the FAS population based on the FDA analyses. Out of 12 responders, 9 were without relapse. Median duration of response was 617 days (95% CI: 185,822 days).

L905: 6.2.2. Historical Literature Review
L906: 6.2.2.1. Design, Literature Historical Review
L907: During the review of the application, FDA requested additional data to justify the 15% response rate in untreated patients to further evaluate the clinical benefit of narsoplimab-wuug in patients with HSCT-TMA to serve as a control group for OMS-TMA-00. The Applicant submitted the literature based historical control on April 23, 2021. Because of Due the extent of the additional review required, a major amendment was issued on May 19, 2021, extending the user fee goal date by 3 months.
L908: Objectives: Describe the natural history of HSCT-TMA in adult patients following allogeneic transplant.
L909: • Describe the demographic and baseline characteristics of adult patients with HSCT-TMA reported in the literature• Describe the literature-reported frequency of HSCT-TMA improvement/resolution in the following adult patient populations:— All reported patients except those noted to have received treatment with eculizumab and/or defibrotide (AP group)— All reported patients in the AP group except those noted to have received any pharmacological treatment (nonpharmacologically treated patients, or NPTP). These excluded therapies: rituximab, vincristine, pravastatin, recombinant thrombomodulin, etanercept, prednisone, mycophenolate mofetil, cyclosporin, imatinib, doxycycline, limaprost. (NPTP group)
L910: Reviewer comment
L911: The withdrawal or change in calcineurin inhibitors as initial management was not characterized or described. This is a limitation of the data because CNI discontinuation is a common step in TMA management.
L912: • Describe the literature-reported 100- day survival following HSCT-TMA diagnosis in the following adult patient populations:— All reported patients except those noted to have received treatment with eculizumab and/or defibrotide group (AP group)— All reported patients except those noted to have received any pharmacological treatment (NPTP group as above)• Describe the impact of the following risk factors on the improvements/resolution of HSCT-TMA and 100- day survival following HSCT-TMA diagnosis:— Co-existing GVHD— Co-existing infection— Co-existing neurological findings— Co-existing pulmonary complications— Co-existing renal dysfunction— Cp-existing gastrointestinal complications— Relapsed or persistent underlying malignancy• Compare the response and 100-day survival rates in the literature- based patient groups to the response and 100-day survival rates observed with narsoplimab-wuug in Study TMA-001.
L913: 6.2.2.2. Eligibility Criteria, Literature Historical Control
L914: The study included patients from the published literature (dated year 20000 and later) only if they met all of the following criteria:
L915: • Age &gt;18 reported in the article• Diagnosis of HSCT-TMA following allogeneic HSCT• Identifiable patient-level data• Data including (i) any variable of the primary response variable (platelet count, lactate dehydrogenase, organ function or transfusions) used in Study TMA-001 or (ii) report of response and at least 1 risk factor present in the narsoplimab-wuug pivotal trial patients (i.e., GVHD, neurological dysfunction, infection, pulmonary dysfunction, renal dysfunction, gastrointestinal dysfunction, relapse, or persistence of underlying malignancy)
L916: Exclusion
L917: • Patients from the published literature were excluded from the study if they had received eculizumab and/or defibrotide therapy.
L918: Data Sources
L919: • The study procedures were limited to review of the published English-language literature generated through a PubMed search.
L920: Methods
L921: The following was used to conduct the literature review:
L922: • Criteria for publication selection were:– Reports of adult patients receiving allogeneic transplants– Reports with patient level data including (i) any component of the primary response endpoint used in the narsoplimab-wuug pivotal trial or (ii) report of response and at least 1 risk factor present in the narsoplimab-wuug pivotal trial patients– Publication after 2000• A PubMed search was conducted of the following terms:– “Thrombotic microangiopathy” and “stem cell– “Transplant-associated thrombotic microangiopathy”– “Cancer associated thrombotic microangiopathy• All unique, English-language articles that met the criteria above were collected.
L923: Primary review of each full publication not abstracts was conducted by at least 2 independent reviewers with medical backgrounds. All articles identified by either reviewer were forward for secondary review by different individuals.
L924: Working independently, 2 physicians performed a secondary review of publications identified in the primary review to confirm correct inclusion of publications in the literature review. Inclusion of publications was based on meeting the criteria for publication selection. A third physician adjudicated any differences.
L925: All relevant data were extracted by one of the secondary review physicians compiled. A medical writer conducted a quality control review on all extracted data against all publications passing the secondary review.
L926: All articles identified in the primary review but rejected in the review were provided. secondary
L927: Operational definitions of the response components from Study TMA-001 and criteria for improvement in the literature patients are:
L928: Platelet Count
L929: Evidence of platelet count improvement was considered a positive platelet count response in the literature patients. Platelet count improvement due to platelet transfusions were not considered a platelet count response in the literature patients.
L930: LDH
L931: Evidence of lactate dehydrogenase improvement was considered a positive LDH response in literature patients
L932: Renal Function
L933: Evidence of creatinine improvement or cessation of RRT was considered a positive renal function response in the literature patients.
L934: Neurological Function
L935: Improvement in gastrointestinal function was defined as any improvement in gastrointestinal function or bleeding reported in the literature patients.
L936: Transfusion Burden
L937: Transfusion freedom was defined as a report in the literature of transfusion freedom in a patient previously receiving transfusions.
L938: Risk Factor Definition
L939: Any of the risk factors of graft-versus-host disease (GHVD), infection, relapse or persistence of underlying malignancy and organ dysfunctions were defined as a report of such complication in the article.
L940: Response
L941: Response were was defined as evidence in the article of reported “response” OR improvement in platelet count, LDH, and either organ function or transfusion burden.
L942: 100-Day Survival
L943: A patient achieved a 100-day survival if the overall survival from the date of HSCT_TMA diagnosis of the patient was \geq 100 days. If the 100-day survival status of a patient was unknown (i.e., censored before 100 days), this patient was considered to have died prior to 100 days.
L944: 6.2.2.3. Statistical Analysis Plan, Literature Historical Review
L945: Sample Size Determination
L946: Not planned
L947: Interim Analyses
L948: Not planned
L949: Missing Data
L950: Different imputations were used to impute missing baseline characteristics and risk factors information for all literature-based patients to enable treatment comparisons between narsoplimab-wuug-treated and literature-control patients. These imputation methods aimed to represent the best- and worst-case scenarios that would cover the actual risk-factor status of the literature-based patients.
L951: The risk-factor status of all narsoplimab-wuug-treated patients was known.
L952: The reported demographics and risk factors from the literature studies were not consistent across studies. In order to perform the propensity score analyses, unknown (i.e., not reported) risk factors for the literature patients were imputed by 4 methods:
L953: • All unknown risk factors are imputed as absent.• All unknown risk factors are imputed as present.• Unknown risk factors are imputed as present for responders, and absent for nonresponders.• Unknown risk factors are imputed as absent for responders, and present for nonresponders.
L954: Exploratory Analyses
L955: The Applicant conducted post-hoc exploratory analyses include propensity score analyses, where propensity scores were estimated by a logistic regression for treatment with the following covariates: age and days from HSCT to TMA diagnosis as continuous variables, and GVHD, infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction as categorical variables (Yes/No). Pulmonary dysfunction, GI dysfunction and relapsed/refractory primary disease were not used because of the large number of unknown (i.e., not reported) statuses in the literature database.
L956: • Model 1 included: Age, days from HSCT to TMA diagnosis, GVHD, infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction• Model 2 included: Age, GVHD, infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction• Model 3 included: Days from HSCT to TMA diagnosis, GVHD, infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction• Model 4 included: GVHD, infection, renal dysfunction, and neurologic dysfunction
L957: Weighted logistic regression for response with a single treatment covariate (narsoplimab-wuug or literature) weighted by stabilized inverse probability of treatment weighting (SIPTW) was performed for each model and imputation method. Odds ratios with 95% CI were calculated. A similar approach was taken for 100-day survival.
L958: Sensitivity analyses using matching were performed on Model 1. 1:k matching (matching k literature patients to 1 narsoplimab-wuug patient, where k = 1 to 4) using a greedy matching method with a caliper of 0.5 (with respect to the pooled standard deviation of the logit of the
L959: propensity score from the 2 treatment groups) was performed for each imputation method. For each matched set and imputation method, a conditional logistic regression for response with a single treatment covariate (narsoplimab-wuug or literature) was performed. Odds ratios with their 95% CIs were calculated.
L960: Diagnostics were performed to evaluate goodness of fit for various models and imputations and to evaluate balancing of risk factors in the matched patient cohorts.

L978: Efficacy Results Literature Review
L979: StudyTMA-001 utilized, endpoint is a composite endpoint which was comprised of an improvement in LDH level and platelet count and a clinical improvement (organ function or freedom from transfusion). There are no standard response criteria for HSCT-TMA and the, collectively a clinically meaningful endpoint and a rigorous test for response to therapy. The literature reports a variety of response criteria, which, for the most part, are not as rigorous as the clinical trial endpoint. We have analyzed these criteria according to a definition of stringency as follows:
L980: Laboratory
L981: Criteria or clinical criterion/criteria only = less stringent compared to Study TMA-001; LDH and platelets and clinical criterion/criteria = “equal” stringency to the narsoplimab-wuug clinical trial endpoint. The response criterion/criteria that were not defined in literature publications were designated as incomplete.
L982: Table 19 summarizes the breakdown for the 35 studies, using the definitions of stringency above, that comprise the AP group and the corresponding response rates. While the NPTP group is most comparable for the narsoplimab-wuug clinical trial population, the AP group, which includes patients receiving therapeutic interventions, is included to provide the most conservative analysis. As can be seen from Table 19, the literature-based response rates across stringency levels range from approximately 29% to 37% in the AP group and 18% to 32% in the NPTP group. For main Study TMA-001, the Applicant estimated response is 61% and FDA estimated response is 43%.

L1005: 6.3. Key Review Issues Relevant to Evaluation of Benefit
L1006: 6.3.1. Determination of Response Rate: Applicant's vs. FDA's analysis
L1007: Issue
L1008: The primary endpoint of Study TMA-001 was the proportion of HSCT-TMA patients who responded to narsoplimab-wuug treatment. There was no control group; therefore, accurate interpretation of the response rate is critical in being able to demonstrate efficacy. Response rate was determined based on improvement in platelets, a reduction in LDH, and clinical improvement. The Applicant concluded that 61% [95% CI: 41%-78%] of patients met the primary endpoint. There are challenges in interpreting the response in the setting of a very complex patient population, disease related issues, endpoint issues and lack of adherence to the protocol. The clinical team reviewed each patient narrative and provided adjudicated responses for the 28 patients. The FDA concluded that the response rate was 43% [95% CI: 24%, 63%].
L1009: Background
L1010: Explanation Regarding Relevance of Review Issue
L1011: The patient population included in Study TMA-001 was complex, consisting of mostly critically ill patients with multiple confounders (i.e., concurrent diseases, concomitant medications), posing a challenge to determine the response rate.
L1012: The clinical review team assessed all 28 patient narratives submitted with the application. What became apparent during the review is that while the primary endpoint had mostly objective components (i.e., platelet count, LDH, serum creatinine, transfusion freedom, increase in haptoglobin) the complexity of the patient population, comorbid illnesses, along with trial design features, impacted the interpretability.
L1013: Specific issues identified that impacted the interpretation of the response to narsoplimab-wuug can be further categorized by disease-related issues, lack of adherence to protocol defined enrollment and response criteria and difficulty in determining magnitude and durability in endpoint (response). These included:
L1014: Disease-Related Issues
L1015: • Concurrent diseases: The majority of patients had a diagnosis of GVHD, significant infection, or other concurrent disease process for which they were actively treated for around the time of HSCT-TMA diagnosis and/or narsoplimab-wuug administration.• Concurrent medications: Patients were receiving concomitant medications that may impact the response to narsoplimab-wuug, such as modification of calcineurin inhibitors or sirolimus, in addition to other therapies or treatments (i.e., plasma therapy, rituximab, and defibrotide).
L1016: Lack of Adherence to Protocol Defined Enrollment and Response Criteria
L1017: • Not all patients who were classified by the Applicant as responders met all three components (platelet, LDH and improvement in organ function (renal, pulmonary, or gastrointestinal) of the primary endpoint definition. deviating from protocol defined response.• A significant number of patients did not meet the predefined inclusion/exclusion criteria but were still included in the study. Magnitude of serum creatinine change may be difficult to interpret for patients who did not meet the inclusion criteria of doubling of serum creatinine from pre-HSCT level.
L1018: Difficulty in Determining Magnitude of Laboratory Changes and Durability of Response
L1019: • The significance of magnitude of change was difficult to assess in patients with LDH just above the ULN.• Difficulty interpreting improvement in neurologic and/or GI dysfunction as these clinical manifestations could be multifactorial and were treated concurrently• Durability of response and time to response
L1020: Assessment
L1021: Disease-Related Issues
L1022: Confounding Concurrent Disease and Treatments
L1023: GVHD and infections are common complications of HSCT. The basis of the pathophysiology of HSCT-TMA involves endothelial injury and microthrombi formation (Khosla et al. 2018). First-line treatment of HSCT-TMA is typically to limit and when possible, completely remove the condition which is causing endothelial injury, which includes treatment of infections and GVHD (Khosla et al. 2018). This may be an effective treatment strategy in particular when HSCT-TMA is mild-moderate (Rosenthal 2016). The clinical review team evaluated the rates of infections and GVHD in Study TMA-001 in order to assess the possible impact of these disease processes on the primary endpoint.
L1024: In total, 19/28 (67.9%) of patients had a diagnosis of GVHD (acute and/or chronic) around the time of HSCT-TMA diagnosis and/or during narsoplimab-wuug treatment. This included 12/17 (70.6%) responders and 7/11 (63.6%) nonresponders with GVHD. Patients were treated with corticosteroids along with other medications used to treat GVHD.
L1025: In addition, most patients had significant infections. This included 23/28 (82.1%) of patients with a significant infection. Table 21 shows significant infections as defined by the Applicant that occurred 2 weeks prior to the first dose of narsoplimab-wuug dose or during the treatment period. Of the responders 15/17 (88.2%) had significant infections and 9/11 (81.8%) nonresponders had significant infections. Patients were treated with antibiotics, antifungals, and/or antivirals depending on the type of infection.
L1026: While the clinical review team acknowledges that these concurrent diseases may increase the risk of poor outcomes, it can be challenging to determine if the treatment impacted the response in the setting of single-arm trial. Notably, responders and nonresponders had similar rates of GVHD and infections.

L1033: Concomitant Medications
L1034: CNI or Sirolimus
L1035: While there are no approved therapies for the treatment of HSCT-TMA, one of the first management steps includes removing the potential offending agent, such as CNIs and/or sirolimus. The Bone Marrow Transplant Clinical Trials Network Toxicity Committee consensus
L1036: summary recommended CNI cessation as the first line management (Ho et al. 2005; Epperla et al. 2020). Not all patients respond to this method of HSCT-TMA management. Replacing CNIs with other immunosuppressive agents has been demonstrated to be more successful than CNI dose reductions (Elsallabi et al. 2016). Experts have recently disputed the effectiveness of CNI or sirolimus dose reductions and discontinuations, however. Recent cohort studies have suggested that immunosuppression withdrawal does not impact patient outcomes (Li et al. 2019; Epperla et al. 2020). Therefore, this issue remains controversial.
L1037: In an information request (IR) response to the Agency, the Applicant reported CNI change in 7/17 (41.2%) of responders and 7/11 (63.6%) of nonresponders. The clinical reviewer conducted a separate analysis by reviewing the 28 narratives for CNI change or discontinuations in both responders and nonresponders. CNI or sirolimus dose reduction or discontinuation was considered relevant if it occurred either within 2 weeks before starting narsoplimab-wuug or during treatment. In total, 10/17 (58.9%) of responders had CNI or sirolimus changes, compared to 3/11 (27.3%) of nonresponders (Table 22).
L1038: While more responders had CNI and/or sirolimus modifications or discontinuations compared to nonresponders, the significance of this is uncertain given there is no control arm in this study.

L1045: Plasma Therapy
L1046: As specified in the protocol, patients were allowed to receive plasma exchange while on study. According the Applicant's CSR, a total 7 patients (25%) received plasma therapy while on study. Per protocol four patients received supplemental doses of narsoplimab-wuug at 2 mg/kg IV while receiving narsoplimab-wuug. Of the patient's that received extra doses of narsoplimab-wuug, 2 (50%) were responders while receiving plasma exchange. While plasma exchange may be an effective treatment for TTP, this mode of therapy is thought to be less beneficial in HSCT-TMA. Plasma exchange has variable response rates and an unclear impact on mortality (Khosla et al. 2018).
L1047: Rituximab
L1048: According to the Applicant's CSR, 5 patients received rituximab as a concomitant medication. Patients were allowed to receive rituximab therapy during the Study TMA-001. In reviewing the concomitant medication dataset (adcm.jmp), 6 patients were identified who received rituximab within the month (30 days) before receiving narsoplimab-wuug and/or during narsoplimab-wuug treatment. Three out of 6 (50%) of patients were responders (Table 23).
L1049: While no large clinical trials have been conducted with rituximab in patients with HSCT-TMA, there has been some sparse data suggesting a role for rituximab as a therapy option for patients with HSCT-TMA (Jodele et al. 2015). An equal number of responders and nonresponders received rituximab, although the overall number of patients receiving rituximab is small, and interpretation may be confounded by the fact that 3 patients received plasma therapy as well. Plasma therapy in combination with rituximab may be effective therapy in some patients (insert reference).
