L1494: Conclusion
L1495: While autoimmunity has been described in the setting of MASP-2 deficiency, the association with narsoplimab-wuug is not clear. Autoimmunity due to MASP-2 deficiency has rarely been described in the literature and low levels of MASP-2 occur in healthy individuals as well without autoimmunity. In addition, there were no apparent TEAEs of autoimmune disease development
L1496: in clinical trials with narsoplimab-wuug. The current proposed dosing is relatively short and will not be a daily or chronic use medication. However, would recommend long-term follow-up of patients treated with narsoplimab-wuug to monitor for late occurring autoimmunity in context of a postmarketing commitment.
L1497: 8. Therapeutic Individualization
L1498: 8.1. Intrinsic Factors
L1499: A population PK model was used to assess the effect of covariates on narsoplimab-wuug exposure. The covariates that affect narsoplimab-wuug exposure included patients, body weight, serum albumin, and presence of ADAs. Compared to healthy subjects, patients had a 20% lower maximum elimination rate (V_{max}). Albumin, particularly at high levels, can result in lower narsoplimab-wuug clearance and greater exposure. Presence of ADA increased V_{max} by 143%, decreased AUC_{ss} by 22%, and decreased C_{min,ss} by 52% (Section 14.3.2). The effect of weight is discussed in the section below and in the Pharmacometrics review (Section 14.3).
L1500: The covariates that did not affect narsoplimab-wuug exposure included age, race, and sex.
L1501: Body Weight
L1502: The Applicant's population PK model showed that body weight was a significant covariate for narsoplimab-wuug clearance and, therefore, proposed, dosing of 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug, up to a maximum of 370 mg, administered by intravenous infusion once weekly. The Office of Clinical Pharmacology assessment concluded that the proposed dosing of 4 mg/kg of narsoplimab-wuug capped to 370 mg is appropriate, as it provides comparable steady-state exposure (area under the concentration-time curve over the dosing interval: AUC_{ss}, maximum concentration: C_{max}, and trough concentration: C_{min}) across all body weight ranges (Figure 27, Section 14.3.2).
L1503: Figure 11. Predicted Narsoplimab-wuug AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss}, and C_{min,ss} for 4 mg/kg and 370 mg in Patients
L1504: Dose: ■ 4 mg/kg ■ 370 mgAUCss (ug*h/mL)Cmax,ss (ug/mL)Weight (kg)Two box plots showing predicted Narsoplimab-wuug exposure (AUCss and Cmax,ss) versus weight (kg) for two dosing regimens: 4 mg/kg (red) and 370 mg (blue). A vertical dashed line at 92.5 kg separates the two groups. For AUCss (left plot), the y-axis ranges from 1000 to 8000 ug*h/mL. For Cmax,ss (right plot), the y-axis ranges from 25 to 100 ug/mL. In both plots, the 4 mg/kg dose generally results in lower exposure than the 370 mg dose for weights above 92.5 kg, while the opposite is true for weights below 92.5 kg.
L1505: Source: FDA Reviewer
L1506: The reviewer’s PK simulations demonstrated that capping narsoplimab-wuug dosing to 370 mg, particularly in heavier subjects, provides consistent and comparable narsoplimab-wuug exposure (mainly C_{max,ss} and AUC_{ss}) to subjects with body weight below 92.5 kg and receiving 4 mg/kg of narsoplimab-wuug. Conversely, the administration of 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug dose in subjects with body weight above 100 kg or administration of a fixed dose of 370 mg in subjects with body weight below 70 kg is expected to result in relatively higher exposure compared to the studied and current proposed dosing.
L1507: 8.2. Extrinsic Factors
L1508: Plasma Therapy/Plasma Exchange
L1509: The objective of plasma therapy (PLT), specifically plasma exchange (PE), in HSCT-TMA patients is to remove thrombogenic and antifibrinolytic molecules and to replenish anticoagulants and profibrinolytics with new plasma. The process removes large molecules such as proteins and monoclonal antibodies from the systemic circulation. (b) (4)
L1510: (b) (4) that an additional dose of 2 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug be administered up to 3 times weekly. However, the Applicant did not provide a justification for the selected supplemental narsoplimab-wuug dose of 2 mg/kg. The effect of PLT on narsoplimab-wuug clearance (CL) and its overall exposure was not investigated in a dedicated clinical study, and the Applicant did not attempt to use a population PK modeling approach to assess the effect of PLT on narsoplimab-wuug exposure or justify the need for the selected supplemental dose of narsoplimab-wuug post-PE.
L1511: Five patients received PE during narsoplimab-wuug treatment, and were used to evaluate the effect of PE on narsoplimab-wuug PK. The median duration of PE in these 5 patients was 1.8 h (range: 1.4 h to 2.9 h). Following an information request (see Section 14.3.5), the Applicant's post hoc analysis estimated that PLT increased narsoplimab-wuug CL by 37.1% based upon their population PK modeling and concluded that due to the modest effect size and short duration of PLT, no clinically meaningful effects of PLT on narsoplimab-wuug exposure could be identified (b) (4).
L1512: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology's review of the population PK analysis found that PE increased narsoplimab-wuug CL by an average of 47%. However, the estimated effect on narsoplimab-wuug CL could not be precisely estimated, with a 95%CI of the mean effect ranging from 24% to 124%. The difficulty in reliably estimating the effect of PE on CL using the population PK model is most likely related to the lack of informative PK data. There was no PK sampling performed concurrently immediately pre- and post-PE amongst the 5 patients who received PE during narsoplimab-wuug treatment.
L1513: The reviewer's PK simulations showed that PE does not seem to have a relevant effect on narsoplimab-wuug PK (see Section 14.3 Pharmacometrics review for more details). However, the population PK modelling was not considered reliable for predicting the effect of plasma therapy on drug exposure. Therefore, the need as well as the strength and frequency of supplemental dosing post-PE could not be reliably evaluated.
L1514: The Applicant and the Clinical Pharmacology review team have shown that PE increases narsoplimab-wuug clearance; therefore, supplementation of narsoplimab-wuug at 2 mg/kg following each PE appears to be reasonable. The supplemental dose of 2 mg/kg is supported by safety established in the clinical trials. However, it is unclear if the supplemental dose and dosing regimen is optimal. To identify a precise dose and dosing regimen for narsoplimab-wuug supplementation following PE, the Office of Clinical Pharmacology review team recommends that Applicant conduct a dedicated clinical study to methodically assess the effect of PE on narsoplimab-wuug clearance and to identify a dose and dosing regimen that can replace narsoplimab-wuug that is removed through PE.
L1515: 8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development
L1516: The initial application for approval of narsoplimab-wuug is limited to adults. Pediatric patients were not included in the Phase 2 Study TMA-001. Considering multiple interactions between the complement system and the immune system and the role of complement in some forms of bone remodeling, a juvenile toxicity study in mice was conducted to support future pediatric development. Mice were administered narsoplimab IV twice a week at doses of 0, 50, 150, or 300 mg/kg for 8 injections during the preweaning period (postnatal day 4-28) followed by subcutaneous (SC) once a week for 9 injections during the postweaning period (postnatal day 35-91).
L1517: Substantial (&gt;70%) and similar inhibition of the complement lectin pathway was observed at all dose levels and was sustained beyond 28 days after the last dose, verifying that all dose levels tested were pharmacodynamically active.
L1518: No treatment-related effects were observed on physical development parameters (vaginal opening and preputial separation), behavioral performance, or bone dimensions. Treatment-related findings included non-adverse increases in neutrophil counts, eosinophilic infiltration in the bone marrow, injection site, and inguinal skin, and moderately elevated IgE plasma levels. These changes showed reversibility and did not affect antibody response to antigen in a T-cell dependent antibody response assessment. The exposure multiple at the NOAEL is 2X MRHD based on estimated AUC(0-168h).

8.1 Pregnancy

Subcutaneous dosing of pregnant female mice had no effect on embryofetal development up to 150 mg/kg at ~3-times the extrapolated AUC0-168h at the MRHD.
L1531: Labeling SectionNonclinical Data





• Intravenous dosing of pregnant mice led to lower number of live fetuses, decreases in fetal body weights up to 4.8%, and an increase of approximately 2-fold in postimplantation loss and the number of litters with cleft palate malformation occurred in mice at ~22-times the exposure at the MRHD. Potential adverse maternal effects were seen as reduced gestational weight gain.
• Subcutaneous dosing of pregnant rabbits had no effect on embryofetal development up to 150 mg/kg or ~8 times the AUC at the MRHD.
• Intravenous dosing of pregnant rabbits (150 mg/kg) increased postimplantation loss and reduced fetal body weights up to 8.5% below controls at 91-times the exposure at the MRHD.
• In a pre- and post-natal development study in mice dosed IV at 300 mg/kg or SC at 150 mg/kg, transiently reduced maternal body weight gain was observed during gestation. There was no narsoplimab-related adverse effect on growth, sexual maturation, or neurobehavioral or reproductive function in the offspring.
• Narsoplimab was present in serum of offspring suggesting maternal transfer in milk during lactation; however, milk levels of narsoplimab were not measured directly.

L1548: III. Appendices
L1549: 12. Summary of Regulatory History
L1550: The following provides a regulatory overview for biologics license application (BLA) 761152, product name OMS721 (also referred to as narsoplimab-wuug). OMS721 is a monoclonal antibody that binds to and blocks mannan-binding lectin-associated serine protease 2, the effector enzyme of the lectin pathway of complement, inhibiting the inflammatory and prothrombotic responses to endothelial injury. The proposed indication is hematopoietic stem cell transplant-associated thrombotic microangiopathy (HSCT-TMA). OMS721 is stored as a single dose yellow/brown solution in a vial to be administered as an intravenous injection. Currently, no Food and Drug Administration (FDA)-approved products are indicated for the treatment of HSCT-TMA.
L1551: On January 23, 2014, the Agency received the submission for an investigational new drug (IND) application under section 505(i) of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act for IND 120524. Prior to the submission a Type B pre-IND meeting was conducted with Omeros Corporation on January 23, 2014, for OMS721 under PIND 120524. The Applicant obtained Agency advice on the development plans for the investigation of OMS721 for inhibition of complement mediated thrombotic microangiopathy (TMA) under Section 351(a) of the Public Health Service Act. On April 2, 2014, Omeros Corporation was advised that the 30-day, safety review of the application had concluded and that the study may proceed with their proposed clinical investigation for the treatment of chemotherapy induced neutropenia with nonhold comments. On September 8, 2014, an institutional review board waiver was granted under 21 CFR Part 56 for the use of OMS721 for the conduct of Study TMA-001 in Germany.
L1552: A type B, end-of-phase 2 meeting was held in person on April 24, 2018, to discuss a Phase 3 program design and the clinical studies to support submission of a BLA for OMS721 in the treatment of HSCT-TMA. The Division stated that the Applicant should provide an explanation for the following:
L1553: • Why OMS721 demonstrated little efficacy in patients with thrombotic thrombocytopenia purpura (TTP) or aHUS.• Why the biweekly or three times a week dosing of OMS721 was given to the compassionate-use patient compared to the weekly dosing used in Study TMA-001.• Why the volume of distribution of OMS721 (0.071 L/kg) is similar to that of blood volume (~0.08 L/kg). The Division requested the Applicant address if changes in volume status (such as hypervolemia due to therapy interventions) may have an impact on the pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic (PK/PD) and/or efficacy of OMS721 in patients with HSCT-TMA.
L1554: On April 26, 2018, breakthrough therapy designation was granted for OMS721 for the treatment of patients with HSCT-TMA. Breakthrough therapy designation was followed by a July 31,
L1555: 2018, Type B – breakthrough therapy meeting. Discussions from this meeting included the following:
L1556: • The Division stated that multiple hurdles would need to be overcome using a chart-based historical control and recommended that the Applicant use TMA response rate as primary endpoint in a single-arm trial with duration of response as a secondary endpoint. The Applicant noted the challenges with a TMA response rate endpoint due to confounding effects of transplantation and other concomitant medical conditions.• The Applicant discussed their plan for conducting a historical control using chart review. The Agency expressed several concerns, to include: ensuring matching populations, contemporaneous review of charts and study patients, data integrity, time involved, endpoint definition, and various aspects of the propensity score analysis.• The Agency stated that they could not agree with the Applicant’s proposal to use a chart based historical control until further review and internal discussion. The Division recommended that the Applicant submit a detailed proposal of the chart based historical control for the Agency to review and provide feedback.
L1557: On January 29, 2019, a Type B meeting was held. Discussion at this meeting included the following:
L1558: • The Division stated that the decision on whether to accept a proposed surrogate endpoint is based on the scientific support for that endpoint and that confirmatory trials are usually ongoing at the time of an application submission.• The Division recommended that the Applicant assemble the data to define a responder endpoint definition for both surrogate and clinical benefit endpoints.• The Division recommended that the Applicant include the scientific rationale for endpoints selected. Additionally, the Applicant should prespecify the endpoints to reduce or minimize potential bias and include discussions of why some response outcomes are excluded from responder endpoint definition.
L1559: On March 2, 2019, a Type A meeting was held to discuss response-based clinical endpoints for a registrational trial. Discussion point(s) for this meeting included:
L1560: • The Agency expressed concerns that the magnitude of change in platelet count based on their current proposal may not be meaningful.• The Agency recommended that the Applicant prespecify an endpoint definition for platelets based on proportion of patients who achieve a prespecified threshold.• The Agency also recommended that the Applicant review data with their drug to determine appropriate threshold for a proposal.
L1561: On March 12, 2019, preliminary comments were issued for a type B, chemistry, manufacturing, and controls (CMC) meeting. The Meeting request was then withdrawn by the Applicant on March 15, 2019, citing acknowledgment and understanding of The Division’s preliminary comments.

L1571: 13. Pharmacology Toxicology: Additional Information and Assessment
L1572: 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted Under the IND
L1573: 13.1.1. Pharmacology
L1574: Primary Pharmacology
L1575: • Narsoplimab-wuug is a monoclonal antibody that binds to and blocks mannan-binding lectin-associated serine protease-2 (MASP-2), the effector enzyme of the lectin pathway of complement, see Figure 12.
L1576: • MASP-2 also reportedly contributes to cleavage of prothrombin to thrombin independent of downstream complement activation (Krarup et al. 2007); see orange arrows/boxes, Figure 12. Narsoplimab was not tested against this reported MASP-2 activity.
• In vitro binding studies using plasmon resonance and enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) showed high affinity binding of OMS721 with human MASP-2 (62-73pM). Intact bivalent OMS721 demonstrated 100-fold greater binding affinity compared to a monovalent Fab indicating that functional bivalency is an important contributor to high-affinity binding, see Table 51.
• OMS721 exhibited a selectivity ratio of &gt;8,000-fold for MASP-2 relative to other serine proteases of the complement system (C1r, C1s, MASP-1, and MASP-3).
• In vitro functional assays demonstrated that OMS721 inhibited human lectin pathway-dependent C3, C4 (IC50 2.5nM), and membrane attack complex (MAC) (IC50 1nM) activation without affecting the classical and alternative pathway-dependent MAC activation at concentrations up to 100nM.
L1577: Figure 12. Proposed Mechanisms of Action of OMS721
L1578: The diagram illustrates the complement system pathways and the proposed mechanism of action of OMS721 (narsoplimab). The pathways shown are:
CLASSICAL PATHWAY: Immune complex → C1q → C1r/C1s → C4 + C2 (C3 convertase) → C3 → C3a (Inflammation, platelet activation, leukocyte recruitment, endothelial cell activation) + C3b → C5 (C5 convertases) → C5a + C5b → C6-9 (Terminal Pathway (C5b-9)) → MAC → Cell lysis.
LECTIN PATHWAY: Tissue injury → MBL, ficolins, collectins → MASP-2. MASP-2 activates C4 + C2 (C3 convertase) → C3 → C3a + C3b. MASP-2 also activates Factor XII → Factor XIa → Prothrombin → Thrombin → Coagulation. Narsoplimab inhibits MASP-2.
ALTERNATIVE PATHWAY: C3 → C3b (via Factor B, Factor D, pro-Factor D, MASP-3) → C5 → C5a + C5b → C6-9 → MAC → Cell lysis.
A dashed arrow labeled "C4 bypass" points from C3b to C5. A solid arrow labeled "C4 bypass" points from C3b to C5.Diagram illustrating the proposed mechanisms of action of OMS721 (narsoplimab) on the complement system. The diagram shows three main pathways: Classical Pathway, Lectin Pathway, and Alternative Pathway. Narsoplimab is shown inhibiting the Lectin Pathway at the MASP-2 step. The Classical Pathway starts with an Immune complex activating C1q, which then activates C1r/C1s. C1r/C1s activates C4 and C2 to form C3 convertase. C3 convertase activates C3 to C3a and C3b. C3b activates C5 to C5a and C5b. C5b activates C6-9 to form the MAC. The MAC leads to cell lysis. The Lectin Pathway starts with Tissue injury activating MBL, ficolins, and collectins, which then activate MASP-2. MASP-2 activates Factor XII to Factor XIa, which activates Prothrombin to Thrombin, leading to coagulation. MASP-2 also activates C4 and C2 to form C3 convertase. The Alternative Pathway starts with Factor B and Factor D activating C3 to C3b. C3b activates C5 to C5a and C5b. C5b activates C6-9 to form the MAC. The MAC leads to cell lysis. Narsoplimab is shown inhibiting the Lectin Pathway at the MASP-2 step, preventing the activation of C4 and C2 to form C3 convertase. The diagram also shows the C4 bypass, where C3b activates C5 to C5a and C5b. The MAC leads to cell lysis. The diagram also shows the terminal pathway (C5b-9) leading to the MAC and cell lysis. The diagram also shows the inflammation, platelet activation, leukocyte recruitment, and endothelial cell activation resulting from C3a and C5a activation.
L1579: Source: Excerpted from the Applicant's submission

L1588: • OMS721 displayed functional activity in mouse, rabbit, and cynomolgus monkey, with potencies 3.8, 2.7 and 10-fold less than human. No functional activity was observed in rats. Functional potency was determined from ELISA and flow cytometric measurement of C4 and C3 activation in serum from each species tested against mannan immobilized on a substrate.
• An ex-vivo and an in vivo pharmacodynamic study showed that narsoplimab reduced platelet/endothelial adherence, and delayed the initiation of thrombus formation and time to complete vessel occlusion providing evidence of its mode of action in conditions with a microvascular thrombotic component, see Section 5.1. Inhibition of the lectin pathway by narsoplimab is evident from the reduction in C5b-9 deposition on endothelial cells; however, other complement and coagulation pathways remain active which ultimately led to similar levels of microvascular occlusion in the murine TMA study regardless of treatment with narsoplimab.
• The minimum pharmacodynamically active dose of OMS721 in mice and monkeys was 5 mg/kg and 0.5 mg/kg, intravenously (IV), respectively. There was a consistent correlation in both species between OMS721 serum concentrations and percent inhibition of lectin pathway activity. Based on PK-PD response modelling, an in vivo EC_{50} value for lectin pathway inhibition in cynomolgus monkey was estimated to be approximately 7 µg/mL.
L1589: Secondary Pharmacology
L1590: • A mouse tail bleeding assay was conducted to evaluate whether MASP-2 inhibition affects hemostasis. A recombinant mouse monoclonal antibody, AbD04211, which binds to mouse MASP-2 and blocks the lectin pathway of complement in mice, was used in this study. MASP-2 blockade did not significantly affect the bleeding time in mice assessed after a standard cut applied to the tail. This result suggests that any contribution of MASP-2 to cleavage of prothrombin, as reported in the literature, is marginal and insufficient to alter the hemostatic or thrombotic responses by this mechanism.
L1591: Safety Pharmacology
L1592: • Cardiovascular and respiratory safety pharmacology assessment in monkeys and CNS safety assessments in mice were incorporated into 28-day repeat dose toxicology studies.
L1593: • OMS712 showed no effect on cardiovascular and respiratory parameters in cynomolgus monkeys or on neurobehavioral parameters in mice.
L1594: 13.1.2. Toxicology
L1595: Study Number/ Title: 1235-013: A 28-Day Subcutaneous and Intravenous Toxicity Study in Mice With a 56-Day Recovery Period
L1596: 10, 50, 150 mg/kg/week subcutaneous (SC), 10 mg/kg/week IV
L1597: Exposure margins
L1598: 0.8, 2 and 4-times (SC) and 1-times (IV) the clinical dose of 4 mg/kg/week IV, based on AUC
L1599: Key study findings
L1600: • Injection site reactions consisting of minimal to mild subacute/chronic inflammation were observed in all groups, including control, but were of greater severity in the dosed groups.
• The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) with IV route is 10 mg/kg given once weekly for 4 weeks.
• The NOAEL with SC route is conservatively 50 mg/kg given once weekly for 4 weeks, based on potential adverse hepatic effects:
      
— Increases in AST (2-fold) and ALT (2.6-fold) in SC treated females (at 150 mg/kg) relative to controls at the end of treatment period (largely a result of changes observed in female animal no. 4509)
— Liver histopathology (necrosis) in animals receiving 150 mg/kg SC (although liver necrosis was also observed in 2 control females).

L1606: ParametersMajor Findings



MortalityOne (incidental)

Clinical signsScabbed areas and red discoloration of the skin was observed at the subcutaneous injection sites on the dorsal surface.

Body weightsNo effects on body weights but food consumption was decreased in animals receiving OMS721 via IV route with some recovery during the 8-week recovery period.

OphthalmoscopyNo effect

Functional observational batteryNo effect

HematologyNo effect

Clinical chemistryAt termination and recovery collections, some females in all OMS721 treatment groups had mild to moderate elevations in AST (up to +9.6-fold in one HD female animal 4509) and occasionally ALT (up to +15.0-fold in one HD female animal 4509), relative to control means. Similar changes were also noted in occasional males receiving 10 mg/kg/IV at the recovery collection (AST up to +2.8-fold; ALT up to +3.6-fold). Increases in ALT and AST in animal 4509 correlated with microscopic changes in the liver (minimal hepatocellular necrosis). The increase in liver enzyme seemed to be dose-related at least in the females.

Gross pathologyNo effect

Organ weightsNo effect

HistopathologyAdequate battery: YesPeer reviewed: Yes
Injection site
          Minimal to mild subacute/chronic inflammation at injection sites were observed in all groups including control but increased incidence and severity were noted in OMS721 treated groups. By the end of recovery period, injection site findings were similar in incidence and severity across all groups including controls.
        


Liver
          Minimal liver necrosis was observed in 2 control females and with a higher incidence of 3 HD males and 2 HD females. No liver findings were observed in recovery groups.
        

Toxicokinetics
TK was evaluated on Days 1 and 22 with blood samples collected at predose, 0.083, 0.5, 2, 6, 24, 72, and 168 hours after dosing.
Exposures to OMS721 (C_{max} and AUC_{0-168\ hour}) increased with increasing SC dose (less than dose proportional, especially at mid to high dose increase).
At 10 mg/kg, systemic exposure was greater following IV bolus as compared to SC administration.
Systemic exposure (C_{max} and AUC_{0-168\ hour}) exhibited accumulation following repeated SC and IV administration (mean accumulation ratios ranged from 1.26 to 2.03).
A single control animal (male 6031) had a quantifiable OMS721 concentration of 682 ng/mL on Day 22 (72 hour), which did not affect interpretation of the results.
Terminal half-lives after SC and IV administration ranged from 216 to 321 hours.

L1610: ImmunogenicityBlood (serum) was collected on Day 1 (168 hours following the first dose), Day 8 (168 hours following the second dose), Day 22 (predose), and on Days 49 (648 hours), and 84 (1488 hours after fourth dose). Of the 532 samples collected, 140 were screened for the presence of antidrug antibodies (ADA). 63 samples screened positive, of which 26 samples were confirmed to be positive for ADA (including 3 samples from the control group).

Pharmacodynamic activitySamples were collected from designated animals on Days 1 (0, 2, and 24 hours post-dose), 22 (2 and 24 hours post-dose), 49 (648 hours) and 84 (1488 hours after the fourth dose). The percent MASP-2 inhibition was generally low (if detectable) in control animals, and in 10 mg/kg SC animals, on Day 1. By Day 22 most of the samples from animals treated with 150 mg/kg SC and 10 mg/kg IV exhibited MASP-2 inhibition on the order of ≥90%. The MASP-2 inhibition was considerably decreased in the recovery samples, but the effect was not entirely absent.

L1626: ParametersMajor Findings



Toxicokinetics
TK was evaluated on Days 1 and 22 with samples collected at pre dose, 0.083, 0.5, 2, 6, 24, 72, and 168 hours after dosing, and on Days 35, 49, 63, and 84.
Exposures to OMS721 (C_{max} and AUC_{0-168\ hour}) were similar in males and females on Days 1 and 22.
The increases in exposures to SC OMS721 (C_{max} and AUC_{0-168hr}) were slightly less than dose proportional to increases in dose.
Systemic exposure (C_{max} and AUC_{0-168hr}) exhibited minimal accumulation following repeated SC (mean accumulation ratios ranged from 1.54 to 1.96)
At 10 mg/kg, systemic exposure was greater following IV slow bolus as compared to SC administration.
Mean bioavailability was ~42.6 and 54.4% on days 1 and 22, respectively.
Mean t_{1/2} calculated for recovery animals following SC and for all animals following IV bolus of OMS721 ranged from 120 to 202 hours.
All control group sample results were below the lower limit of quantification (LLOQ, &lt;391 ng/mL).

L1629: Immunogenicity
Blood (serum) was collected on Day 8 (336 hours following the first dose), Day 22 (168 hours post-dose), and on Days 35, 49, 63, and 84.
Antidrug antibodies were observed sporadically in only a few animals (5 of 40 OMS721 treated monkeys) across dose groups.
In the IV dose group (10 mg/kg/week), ADAs were detected in 3 of 10 monkeys, but when present corresponded with decreased serum OMS721 concentration. In the SC groups, there was no clear effect of ADA status on serum OMS721 concentration.


Pharmacodynamic activity
Samples were collected from designated animals on Days 1 and 22 (at pretest, 0.5 and 24 hours post-dose) and on Days 49 and 77 during the recovery period. Percent inhibition of serum lectin pathway activity (surrogate for MASP-2 activity) was correlated to OMS721 serum concentrations. Maximal inhibition was evident in samples taken from animals given 150 mg/kg SC and 10 mg/kg IV.

L1644: ParametersMajor Findings



Clinical chemistry
A nondose dependent minimal decrease was observed in total protein (-2.7 to -9.5% compared with control) and albumin (-4.4 to -11% compared with control) on Days 91 and 183 of the dosing phase in females at &gt;50 mg/kg IV or SC.
1 female (I03200) in 150 mg/kg IV group showed the most pronounced decreases in total protein (-7 to -9% compared to predose) and albumin (-16 to -28%) on Days 91 and 183 of the dosing phase which became more severe during the recovery phase (-28% total protein and -53% albumin). This female also showed increased WBC and absolute neutrophil counts (3-fold of predose values) at the end of the recovery phase. However, microscopic evidence of inflammation was not observed in the tissues.
All other animals showed reversibility in serum protein changes at the end of the recovery phase.


UrinalysisNo effect

Gross pathologyNo effect

Organ weightsNo effect

HistopathologyAdequate battery: YesPeer reviewed: Yes
OMS721-related minimal to slight mononuclear cell infiltrates were observed at SC injection sites of most animals at all doses. Injection sites reaction showed no dose-response relationship to incidence or severity and showed reversibility at the end of recovery period.
No OMS721-related microscopic findings were seen in animals given 150 mg/kg IV.


Toxicokinetics
TK was evaluated on Days 1, 85 and 176 with samples collected at pre dose, 0.083, 0.5, 2, 6, 24, 72, and 168 hours post-dose. Additionally, samples were collected from all dose groups 10 minutes predose and 72 hours post-dose on Days 29, 57, 120 and 148 and during the recovery phase on Days 28, 56 and 71.
No remarkable sex differences in OMD721 exposures were observed. OMS721 exposure increased less than dose proportionally with SC route. Accumulation with SC route ranged from 1.2 to 4.85. Accumulation was minimal with IV route (0.9-1.8). Mean half-life values ranged from 191-367 hours with SC and 208-300 hours with IV route on Day 176. Mean bioavailability was ~40, 90 and 60% on days 1, 85 and 176, respectively.

L1648: Immunogenicity Blood samples were collected on Days 10 and 17 of the predose phase; predose on Days 29, 57, 85, 120, 148 and 176 of the dosing phase; on Days 28, 56, and Day 71 of the recovery phase. Antidrug antibodies were observed in only 2 of 40 OMS721 treated monkeys. There was no effect of ADA status on serum OMS721 concentration.
L1649: Pharmacodynamic activity Blood samples were collected on Days 10 and 17 of the predose phase; 24 hours post-dose on Day 1; and predose and 72 hours post-dose on Days 29, 57, 85, 120, 148 and 176 of the dosing phase; on Days 28, 56, and Day 71 of the recovery phase. Percent inhibition of serum lectin pathway activity (surrogate for MASP-2 activity) correlated to OMS721 serum concentrations at all doses. There was no effect of ADA status on OMS721 PD activity.

L1652: Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted to the NDA
L1653: Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L1654: Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
L1655: Study Number/Title: 20050868 / NARSOPLIMAB: A Fertility and General Reproduction Toxicity Study by Subcutaneous and Intravenous Injection in Mice
L1656: GLP compliance: Yes
L1657: Key study findings:
L1658: • Body weight gain was 71% lower compared to controls during the overall premating period in 300 mg/kg (IV) females.
• Significant increase in percent abnormal, detached head and no-head sperm parameters occurred in 300 mg/kg (SC/IV) males at 5.4-times the exposure at the MRHD with no direct effects on fertility. MASP-2 is not known to be expressed in testicular tissue (Atlas).
L1659: • Reproductive NOAEL of OMS721 was 300 mg/kg SC for male and female mice at ~7- and ~12-times, respectively, the exposure at the MRHD, based on the lack of a functional effect on fertility.
• Inhibition of the lectin pathway occurred at all doses through the end of the study.

L1664: Table 62. Observations and Results, Fertility and Early Embryonic Development (FEED) Study in Mice
L1665: ParametersMajor Findings



MortalityNo narsoplimab-related mortality occurred in the study.

Clinical signsPiloerection was observed in single occasions in 3/25 females receiving 300 mg/kg IV on SD 15; one of those female mice also presented ptosis (dropping eyelid) on SD 1.

Body weights
Males: Mean body weight gains for Days 1 through 57 compared to control males were +13, +13, +23, and -6% in the LD, MD, and HD (SC) and in the HD (IV), respectively.
Females:
Days 1 to 15, premating period: Mean body weight gains during the overall premating period interval of SD 1 through 15 compared to control females were -29, +43, +57, and -71% in the LD, MD, and HD (SC) and in the HD (IV), respectively.
GD 0 through GD 13: Mean maternal body weight gains during GD 0 through 13 compared to controls were +2, -4, -1, and +4% in the LD, MD, and HD (SC) and in the HD (IV), respectively.


Sperm evaluations (motility, density, and morphology)The mean numbers of normal sperm were reduced or significantly reduced (↓1.9%, ↓4.6%) and the mean percentages of abnormal sperm (↑47%, ↑77%) and mean numbers of sperm with detached heads (↑57%, ↑84%) and no heads (↑47%, ↑76%) were increased or significantly increased (p≤0.05 or p≤0.01) compared to controls. Except for the mean number of sperm with no heads at 300 mg/kg SC, these values were also outside the range of historical control data for the Testing Facility.

L1669: Bold - Data shown in bold indicates test article-related effect.
L1670: Overall, the increase in mean number of sperm with detached head was minimal at the LD SC and dose-related at the MD and HD and therefore, it is considered narsoplimab-related.
L1671: Estrous cycling, mating and fertilityNo narsoplimab-related effects on estrous cycling in females at any dose level. There were no narsoplimab-related effects on days of cohabitation before mating was confirmed, mating index, or fertility index.

Pregnancy parameters (ovarian, uterine, litter observations)
Pregnancy was confirmed in 21, 24, 23, 24, and 21 females in the control, LD, MD and HD (SC) and in the HD (IV), respectively.
No narsoplimab-related effects on any ovarian or uterine parameters (litter means for corpora lutea, implantations, percent preimplantation loss, viable and nonviable embryos, and percent postimplantation loss) at any dose level and no placenta abnormalities detected.


Necropsy findingsA single 300 mg/kg IV female presented a tan area in the liver left lateral lobe and caudate lobe and a large spleen. The relation of these findings to narsoplimab was unclear based on a single occurrence (1/24 pregnant females).

ToxicokineticsSystemic exposure to OMS721 following SC administration generally increased with increasing dose in a dose proportional manner, except for males between 50
L1672: Parameters Major Findings
and 150 mg/kg SC and between 50 and 300 mg/kg SC on SD 57, where exposure was less than dose proportional.
Following repeated dosing, accumulation was observed for males following both routes of administration. For females, accumulation was observed at 50 mg/kg SC and at 300 mg/kg IV.
Exposure was similar among genders and it was higher for both sexes after IV compared to the SC route on all occasions. The absolute bioavailability was similar between males and females and ranged from 47.1% to 68.3% for AUC(0-t).

L1686: Embryo Fetal Development
L1687: Study Number/Title: 20050865 / Narsoplimab: An Embryo-Fetal Development Study by Subcutaneous and Intravenous Injection in Pregnant Mice
L1688: GLP compliance: Yes
L1689: Reviewer's Comment
L1690: Subcutaneous administration provided the more relevant data as it utilized the clinical route of administration and achieved a modest ~3-fold multiple of clinical AUC exposure. The intravenous route led to maternal and fetal toxicity associated with a very high Cmax and AUC relative to clinical exposure and to mice from SC dosing.
L1691: Key study findings:
L1692: Subcutaneous administration:
L1693: • There were no clear drug-related adverse findings in dams or fetuses with subcutaneous administration.
• The maternal and fetal NOAEL at 300 mg/kg SC provided ~3-times the exposure at the MRHD, narsoplimab based on extrapolated AUC0-168h.
L1694: Intravenous administration
L1695: • Lower gestational weight gain and a single early decedent (gestation day (GD) 15) found with a litter of 13 dead fetuses suggests maternal toxicity was present at the 300 mg/kg IV dose (~22-times AUC and ~150-times Cmax relative to MRHD)• Embryofetal loss is indicated by findings of 13 dead fetuses from the early decedent female, a ~2-fold higher percent postimplantation loss, and a lower number of live fetuses.• Surviving fetuses had lower body weight (-5.1% males, -3.8% females) and an increased number with cleft palate which exceeded the historical control data.

L1700: Table 68. Observations and Results, Embryo-Fetal Developmental (EFD) Study in Mice
L1701: ParametersMajor Findings



Mortality
A 50 mg/kg SC female delivered on GD 18 and it was subsequently euthanized. This female received all 4 doses of narsoplimab, gained weight during the study, did not present clinical signs prior to parturition neither abnormalities at necropsy examination. The litter consisted of 15 live fetuses and 1 early resorption in utero. One fetus presented a supernumerary rib, a common variation.
A 300 mg/kg IV female was found dead after dose administration on GD 15. This dam received all 4 doses of narsoplimab, gained weight during the study, did not present clinical signs prior to death neither abnormalities at necropsy examination. The litter consisted of 13 dead fetuses and one late resorption in utero. The viability of fetuses at the time of early maternal death could not be determined.


Clinical signsNo narsoplimab-related clinical signs occurred on study.

Body weightsMean maternal body weight gains during the interval of GD 6 through 18 were +5%, +7%, +12%, and -5% compared to controls in the 50, 150, and 300 mg/kg SC and the 300 mg/kg IV dose group, respectively. Transient decreased maternal body weight gains were observed in 300 mg/kg IV individual dams at 5.8-times the exposure at the MRHD.

Necropsy findingsNo narsoplimab-related maternal gross findings.

Cesarean section data

Laparohysterectomy EvaluationsThere were no differences on the number and litter averages for corpora lutea and number of implantations; however, pregnant mice receiving narsoplimab at 300 mg/kg IV delivered a lower number of live fetuses and the mean percent postimplantation loss was increased ~2-fold.

Major Findings
L1721: Toxicokinetics
L1722: Systemic exposure to narsoplimab, as measured by C_{max} and AUC_{(0-72)}, generally increased with increasing dose from 50 to 300 mg/kg SC but the increases were less than dose-proportional and exposure values decreased on GD 15 compared to GD 6. Narsoplimab exposure after 300 mg/kg IV administration was at least 7-fold increase compared to the SC administration. There were no signs of accumulation after four doses.

L1736: Study Number 20050867 / NARSOPLIMAB: An Embryo-Fetal Development Study by Subcutaneous and Intravenous Injection in Pregnant Rabbits
L1737: GLP compliance: Yes
L1738: Reviewer's Comment
L1739: A staggered dosing design was employed to accommodate a robust antidrug antibody (ADA) response identified in preliminary studies. Lectin inhibition by narsoplimab was confirmed in the course of the definitive study. Subcutaneous administration provided the more relevant data as it utilized the clinical route of administration and achieved an ~8-fold multiple of clinical AUC exposure. The intravenous route did not result in clear maternal toxicity but was potentially associated with an increase in postimplantation loss associated with a very high C_{max} and AUC relative to clinical exposure.
L1740: Key study findings:
L1741: Subcutaneous administration:
L1742: • There were no clear drug-related adverse findings in dams or fetuses with subcutaneous administration. Transient reductions in maternal weight gain were not considered as indicative of maternal toxicity by the reviewer.• The maternal and fetal NOAEL at 150 mg/kg SC provided ~8-times the exposure at the MRHD, narsoplimab based on extrapolated AUC_{0-168h}.
L1743: Intravenous administration:
L1744: • Increased postimplantation loss occurred on GD 7/10 (+88%) and GD 11/14 (+41%) but not on GD 15/18 at ~91- times the exposure at the MRHD (AUC). This finding is consistent with a drug action during early gestation.

L1752: Table 75. Observations and Results, Embryo-Fetal Developmental (EFD) Study in Rabbits
L1753: ParametersMajor Findings



Mortality
Cohort 1 Dosed GD 7 and 10
A 50 mg/kg/dose SC aborted on GD 23 and then euthanized. Litter of 14 conceptuses (14 empty implantation sites were observed due to abortion). The Applicant considered this abortion not narsoplimab-related since it occurred at the low dose, was not dose-dependent, and the single event was within historical data for the Testing Facility.
Cohort 2 Dosed GD 11 and 14
No abortions or mortality occurred.
Cohort 3 Dosed GD 15 and 18
A 150 mg/kg SC dam aborted on GD 28 and then euthanized. Litter of 9 live and 1 dead fetuses. All fetuses appeared normal at gross external examination. The dead fetus had a small right eye, a dilated aortic arch, a ventricular septal defect proximal to the semilunar valves, and a narrow pulmonary trunk at soft tissue examination. It also had an enlarged frontal suture, fused 4th and 5th sternebra, and a misshapen 1st sternebra. One of the live fetuses had unossified pubis at skeletal examination. All other fetuses appeared normal at soft tissue and skeletal examination. The Applicant considered this abortion as not narsoplimab-related since the occurrence was not dose-dependent and the single event was within historical data for the Testing Facility.


Clinical signs
Cohort 1 Dosed GD 7 and 10
The 50 mg/kg SC rabbit that aborted on GD 23 presented ungroomed fur (GD 17 through 23), absent feces (GD 18 through 20), decreased fecal output (GD 21 through 23), and aborted red tissue and red liquid in the cage pan (GD 23).
Narsoplimab-related clinical signs occurred at 150 mg/kg SC and at 300 mg/kg IV and included soft feces (MD 4/20; HD 3/20), liquid feces (HD 2/20), reduced fecal size (MD &amp; HD 1/20), decreased fecal output (LD 1/20; MD 7/20; HD 2/20), and absent feces (LD &amp; HD single dams). These clinical signs occurred after dose administration and were transient (up to 4 days). Decreased fecal output and absent feces were also observed in one 50 mg/kg SC dam.
Cohort 2 Dosed GD 11 and 14
No narsoplimab-related clinical signs occurred.
Cohort 3 Dosed GD 15 and 18
No adverse clinical signs were observed in the 150 mg/kg SC rabbit that aborted on GD 28. No narsoplimab-related clinical signs occurred in other rabbits.
L1754: ParametersMajor Findings



Body weights
Note from Reviewer: The Applicant called for (b) (4) based on the transient lower mean body weight gain that corresponded with reduction in mean maternal food consumption during the dosing period. The reviewer considered this is not the case based on the nature of rabbit's food consumption, lower mean body weight gain before dosing that occurred in Cohorts 2 and 3, the sporadic lower mean food consumption before, during and after the dosing period at different dose levels and cohorts, and the similar final mean body weight among all groups compared to controls on GD 29, see Tables 76-81 below. L1781: ParametersMajor Findings



Necropsy findingsCesarean section data
Cohort 1 Dosed GD 7 and 10A single 150 mg/kg SC dam presented both kidneys with clear, fluid-filled cysts that were too numerous to count ranging in size from pinpoint to 0.4 cm x 0.5 cm x 0.2 cm. The cysts extended into the renal parenchyma. This gross lesion did not affect pregnancy (9 live fetuses) and was not considered related to OMS721 based on a single incidence in the absence of dose-relationship.
Cohort 2 Dosed GD 11 and 14A single incidence of reduced to a ligament of the right uterine horn in a 50 mg/kg SC dam was not considered narsoplimab-related based on the single incidence and lack of dose-relationship.
Cohort 3 Dosed GD 15 and 18No maternal gross observations occurred in cohort 3.


Laparohysterectomy evaluations
Cohort 1 Dosed GD 7 and 10The number and percentage of dams with any resorptions was increased at the HD (9/47 vs. Control 4/20) but it did not affect the mean number of live fetuses in narsoplimab-treated rabbits.
The mean percent implantation loss was increased at the 50 mg/kg SC (+124%) and 150 mg/kg IV (+88%) but it was lower than control at the 150 mg/kg SC (-33%); the relation of implantation loss to narsoplimab administration is unclear based on the lack of dose-response for SC administration but the increased postimplantation loss at 150 mg/kg IV was considered related to narsoplimab.
Cohort 2 Dosed GD 11 and 14Narsoplimab-treated rabbits presented a better profile compared to control except for an increased mean percent postimplantation loss (+41%) at 150 mg/kg IV that was considered narsoplimab-related.
Cohort 3 Dosed GD 15 and 18Narsoplimab-treated rabbits presented a better profile compared to control except for lower number of females pregnant at 150 mg/kg SC (18/20) and a lower number of live fetuses at the 50 mg/kg SC (153, -20.7%) and 150 mg/kg SC (167, -13.5%) compared to control that corresponded with lower number of corpora lutea (177 and 180 vs. 216) and implantations (162 and 172 vs. 208) although values were within the historical control data range.


Necropsy findingsOffspring
Fetal Malformations/ Variations (external, visceral, skeletal)No narsoplimab-related gross external, soft tissue, or skeletal abnormalities occurred at any dose in all Cohorts in rabbits.
All ossification site averages were similar across all dose groups, including controls.
L1782: ParametersMajor Findings
L1783: Toxicokinetics
L1784: Increases in exposure were less than dose-proportional after the first dose and close to dose proportional after the second dose. The mean exposures were comparable among the 3 TK cohorts at both SC dose levels. OMS721 exposure after 150 mg/kg IV administration was \geq 5-fold increases compared to the SC administration. Exposure after the second dose increased only in TK cohort 2.
L1785: Narsoplimab half-life of 57.7 hours was estimated for the SC route from the first TK cohort and between 14.5 and 22.4 hours for the IV route from all three TK cohorts. Bioavailability (%F) based on AUC(0-72) ranged from 6.77 to 9.27% following the first 150 mg/kg SC dose and ranged from 8.59 to 18.7% following the second SC dose.

L1789: ParametersMajor FindingsAntidrug antibody assessmentsSummary of ADA response• Across all TK Cohorts, 9 of 26 pregnant narsoplimab-treated animals were ADA positive at the last sample evaluated (72 or 96 hours after the last dose).• Across all main cohorts, 12 of 173 pregnant narsoplimab-treated animals were ADA positive at the prefirst dose time point.• None of 173 pregnant narsoplimab-treated animals were ADA positive at 24-hour post second dose• On DG 29, 11 to 19 days after the last dose (depending on cohort), 167 of 173 pregnant narsoplimab-treated animals were ADA positive.• Confirmed positive ADA responses detected at 72 hours after the second dose were generally associated with a decreased serum narsoplimab concentrations and samples were excluded from TK analysis.• Pregnant females positive for ADA at 72 hours presented decreased in pharmacodynamic activity.• Maternal ADA response did not appear to affect fetal narsoplimab serum concentrations in both SC and IV bolus groups.

L1798: Prenatal and Postnatal Development (PPND)
L1799: Study Number/Title: 20113153 / NARSOPLIMAB: A Developmental and Perinatal/Postnatal Reproduction Study by Subcutaneous and Intravenous Injection in the Mouse, Including a Postnatal Behavioral/Functional Evaluation
L1800: GLP compliance: Yes
L1801: Reviewer Comment
L1802: No clear adverse effects were observed in dams or in F1 offspring after administering narsoplimab to dams during gestation and lactation by either the IV or SC routes. Signals of embryofetal toxicity (e.g., cleft palate, fewer live fetuses/litter) observed at 300 mg/kg IV in the definitive embryo-fetal development study were not observed at the same IV dose in this study, for uncertain reasons.
L1803: Key study findings:
L1804: • Narsoplimab-related effects in F0 generation mice were limited to transient and nonadverse reductions in maternal body weight and gestational weight gain at 300 mg/kg IV and 150 mg/kg SC.• There was no effect on litter parameters including body weight and viability indices, and no necropsy findings in preweaning F1 offspring.• No narsoplimab-related effects on growth, sexual maturation, or neurobehavioral or reproductive function in the F1 generation at any dose level evaluated.• Narsoplimab was present in serum of F1 offspring, presumably via transfer during the lactation period. Levels of narsoplimab in maternal milk were not measured.• A NOAEL was defined as 300 mg/kg IV and 150 mg/kg SC.

L1806: ParameterMethod DetailsDose and frequency of dosing:SC: 0 or 150 mg/kg/dose; IV: 0, 150, or 300 mg/kg/doseBIW, Gestation: GD 6, 9, 12, 15, and GD 19, and 22 (mice that did not deliver)Lactation: LD 1, 4, 7, 10, 13, 16, and 19 (for mice that deliver a litter)Route of administration:SC or IVFormulation/vehicle:20mM sodium acetate, 50 mg/mL sorbitol, and 0.01% (v/v) Tween® 80 in sterile water for injection, USP, pH 5.0Species/strain:Mouse / Cr:CD1(ICR)Number/sex/group:25 females/group; F0 generation virgin female mice, 70 or 71 days of age, were cohabitated maximum 4 or 5 days with untreated breeder male mice of the same source and strain, one male mouse per female mouse.LactationDay 1 of lactation (postnatal) was defined as the day of birth and was also the first day on which all pups in a litter were individually weighed.Litters were not culled during the lactation period because random selection of pups for culling could result in potential biases in pup viabilities and body weight gains during this period.WeaningAll F1 generation mice were weaned on PND 21. L1818: Toxicokinetics
L1819: Systemic exposure to narsoplimab on GD 15 increased with increasing IV BIW doses from 150 to 300 mg/kg in a dose proportional manner. The half-life was estimated between 13.8 and 16.2 hours. The V_d suggested that narsoplimab was distributed beyond the vasculature.

L1830: Juvenile Animal Studies
L1831: Study Number/Title: 9000601: A 13-Week Subcutaneous Injection Toxicity Study of in the Juvenile Mouse with a 16-Week Recovery
L1832: Key study findings:
L1833: • Narsoplimab-related nonadverse increases in neutrophil counts at \geq 50 mg/kg corresponded with nonadverse microscopic findings of eosinophilic infiltration in the bone marrow, injection site, and inguinal skin. All changes were resolved at the end of recovery except for lower incidence of findings at the injection site.
• Elevated IgE plasma levels occurred at all dose levels with partial reversibility at the end of the recovery period indicative of an immunological sensitization of juvenile mice to narsoplimab. T-cell dependent antibody response assessment shows juvenile mice successfully mounted an immune response to keyhole limpet hemocyanin.
• Substantial (&gt;70\%) and similar inhibition of the complement lectin pathway was observed at all narsoplimab dose levels. The inhibition was sustained beyond 28 days after the last dose. Results indicate narsoplimab inhibition was achieved at the lowest dose tested.
• Antidrug antibody response onset occurred after the last dose and did not interfere with toxicokinetic assessments of narsoplimab in juvenile mice.
• NOAEL was 300 mg/kg/dose SC at 2-times the exposure at the MRHD (AUC, conservatively corrected for a 4-fold potency difference).

Preweaning period (PND 29): decreases in neutrophil, lymphocyte, monocyte, and/or WBC in males and females at ≥50 mg/kg, and minimal transient decreases in eosinophil counts in males at 300 mg/kg and females at ≥150 mg/kg were noted.

Postweaning period (PND 95): changes observed at PND 29 were resolved. However, neutrophil counts increased at ≥50 mg/kg and corresponded with microscopic findings of eosinophilic infiltration in the bone marrow, injection site and inguinal skin.

End of recovery (PND 207): Full resolution of all changes noted.

L1864: ParametersMajor Findings



Plasma IgE Biomarker of Allergic Reaction
L1865: Dose-related increases in IgE levels at 150 and 300 mg/kg/dose reached statistical significance on PND 29. Levels continued increasing through the postweaning period and reached statistical significance at \geq 50 mg/kg on PND 95 with a maximal fold change of 36.2-fold for one female at 300 mg/kg and of 49.5-fold for one male at 50 mg/kg.
L1866: Partial recovery of the increased IgE concentrations was observed on PND 207.
L1867: Elevated IgE plasma levels were indicative of an immunological sensitization of the mice to narsoplimab.

L1884: T-cell dependent antibody response (TDAR) assessment
Keyhole limpet hemocyanin (KLH) solution was administered on PNDs 81 and 88 (14 and 7 days before the scheduled euthanasia) by intraperitoneal injection to two cohorts of TDAR animals (10/sex/group/cohort).
The kinetics of the anti-KLH IgM and IgG responses indicate that these animals successfully mounted an immune response to KLH.


Sexual maturation and reproductive capacityNo effects on the development of hallmarks for physical development of preputial separation in males and vaginal opening in females at all dose levels.

CNS/ neurobehavioral assessment
Functional observation battery (FOB) assessments were conducted on a subset of mice assigned for recovery on PND 59, 60, or 61, and at the end of the recovery period on PND 199, 200, or 201.
No narsoplimab-related effects on qualitative and quantitative parameters evaluated at any dose level.


Bone evaluationNo changes in bone measurements including right femoral length and width.

Gross pathologyNone

Organ weightsNone

L1886: Adequate battery: Yes
L1887: End of Dosing
L1888: Bone marrow: minimal to mild increased cellularity in eosinophils without a dose-response at \geq 50 mg/kg in both sexes with an increased incidence and severity in HD females compared to males.
L1889: Injection site: minimal to mild eosinophilic infiltration and mononuclear cell infiltration in control and narsoplimab-treated animals that were considered narsoplimab-related based on the increased incidence and severity compared to controls.
L1890: Skin: higher incidence of subcutaneous eosinophilic infiltration, graded as minimal to mild, in females at \geq 50 mg/kg and considered an extension of the findings at the injection site.

Major Findings
L1903: Toxicokinetics
L1904: There was no significant increase in systemic exposure by increasing the dose from 150 to 300 mg/kg SC.
L1905: Narsoplimab accumulated after repeat-dose with ratios for AUC(0-72h) averaging 5X for the 50 and 150 mg/kg and average of 3.7 for the 300 mg/kg but much lower accumulation at the end of the weekly dosing schedule for AUC(0-168h) with average of 2.5X for the MD and &lt;2X for the LD and HD.

L1908: ParametersMajor Findings



Antidrug-antibody response
No ADA was detected predose on PNDs 4, 28, or 91, or following administration of narsoplimab on PND 28.
On PND 91, ADA positive responses were confirmed in 7/89, 8/90, and 3/87 mice at 50, 150, and 300 mg/kg, respectively.
The onset of ADA positive samples was on or after 1344 hours following the last dose, for which all their corresponding TK samples had nonquantifiable concentrations of serum narsoplimab.
Therefore, all ADA confirmed positive results had no impact on the overall TK interpretation and no serum narsoplimab concentrations were excluded from TK analysis.


Pharmacodynamic assessment – inhibition of the lectin pathway
In general, narsoplimab inhibition was similar in male and female juvenile mice.
Inhibition of &gt;70% was frequently observed in all groups after dose administration on PND 91 and was sustained up to 28 days (672 hours) into the recovery period.
Inhibitory activity decreased overtime, but it was \geq 50\% at the end of the 16-week recovery period (2688 hours).
Narsoplimab inhibition of the complement lectin pathway was similar at the LD of 50 mg/kg/dose compared to the MD and HD of 150 and 300 mg/kg/dose.
L1909: Source: Study Report # 9000601
L1910: Abbreviations: ADA, antidrug antibody; HD, high dose; JAS, juvenile animal studies; LD, low dose; MD, mid dose; NK, natural killer; PND, postnatal day; SC, subcutaneous; TK, toxicokinetic

L1914: 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1915: Narsoplimab IV and SC Single Ascending Dose (OMS721-NHV-001)
L1916: Study Design
L1917: Study OMS721-NHV-001 was a Phase 1, sequential, single dose, escalation study to evaluate the pharmacokinetics (PK), pharmacodynamics (PD), and ADA response of narsoplimab-wuug or placebo given via IV and SC administration in healthy male subjects.
L1918: There were 7 cohorts with weight-based dosing of narsoplimab-wuug administered via IV infusion (30 min) or SC injection. The doses were 0.025, 0.675, and 2 mg/kg for IV administration and 0.075, 0.225, 0.675, and 2 mg/kg for SC administration.

L1938: Conclusions
L1939: For the IV route, there was approximately dose proportional increases in C_{max} and AUC_{0-inf}. Clearance (CL) was the same for the three IV doses evaluated. For the SC route of administration, there was greater than dose proportional increase for AUC_{0-inf} and dose proportional for C_{max}.
L1940: For highest single dose 2 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug IV, the median (range) T_{max} was approximately 0.5 (0.5-8.0) h and mean (range) half-life was 79 (44-104) h. At the 2 mg/kg dose, IV administration resulted in approximately 3.1-fold higher exposure (AUC_{0-inf}) and approximately 10-fold higher maximum concentration (C_{max}), compared to SC administration. Narsoplimab-wuug
L1941: Inhibition of the lectin pathway as assessed by C4d inhibition was considered high (80% from baseline) for 0.675 and 2 mg/kg for up to 72 h postdose and highest (~100%) 0.5 to 4 h postdose. The inhibition following lowest IV dose of 0.25 mg/kg was moderate (50-80% inhibition) on Day 1 and declined to baseline afterwards. SC administration resulted in a lower C4 inhibition, compared to IV dosing.
L1942: Narsoplimab-wuug IV and SC Multiple Ascending Dose (OMS721-NHV-002)
L1943: Study Design
L1944: Study OMS721-NHV-002 was a Phase 1, placebo-control, sequential, multiple dose escalation study to evaluate the pharmacokinetics (PK), pharmacodynamics (PD), and ADA response of narsoplimab-wuug or placebo given via IV and SC administration in about 90 healthy male and female subjects. The objectives of the study were to characterize multiple dose pharmacokinetics, assess safety, pharmacodynamic markers of lectin pathway inhibition, and development of antidrug antibody.
L1945: The purpose of including both daily and weekly administration regimens was to assess the potential of providing trough serum narsoplimab-wuug concentrations that can adequately inhibit the lectin pathway. The Applicant anticipated that once daily administration would result in higher trough and lower peak serum narsoplimab-wuug concentrations and a decrease in intersubject variability, compared to weekly administration.
L1946: There were 10 cohorts, two dosing schemes (weight-based dosing or fixed dosing), and two routes of administration (IV or SC).
L1947: Cohorts 1-6 received IV or SC once weekly administration of narsoplimab-wuug or placebo for 6 weeks:
L1948: • Cohort 1: IV once weekly doses of 2 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo• Cohort 2: IV once weekly doses of 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo• Cohort 3: SC once weekly doses of 2 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo• Cohort 4: SC once weekly doses of 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo
L1949: BLA 761152
L1950: Narsoplimab-wuug
L1951: • Cohort 5: SC once weekly doses of 6 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo• Cohort 6: SC once weekly doses of 8 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo
L1952: Cohorts 7 and 8 received SC daily with fixed doses administration narsoplimab-wuug or placebo for 3 weeks
L1953: • Cohort 7: SC daily doses of 50 mg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo• Cohort 8: SC daily doses of 150 mg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo
L1954: Cohorts 9 and 10 received SC once weekly with fixed doses administration of narsoplimab-wuug or placebo for 6 weeks
L1955: • Cohort 9: SC once weekly doses of 370 mg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo• Cohort 10: SC once weekly doses of 740 mg narsoplimab-wuug or placebo

L1970: Conclusions
L1971: Steady-state serum narsoplimab-wuug concentrations were reached by the third (Day 15) IV once weekly injection of 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug. Mean (range) accumulation ratio for 4 mg/mL IV once weekly was 1.3 (1.0-1.8) based upon Day 36/Day 1 serum concentrations.
L1972: Narsoplimab-wuug 2 and 4 mg/kg administered intravenously once weekly (represented as blue and green triangles in Figure 20) resulted in the greatest serum concentration over the 6 weeks of treatment. Subcutaneous dosing with fixed doses or weight-based dosing resulted in lower systemic exposure.
L1973: The Applicant postulated that inhibition of C4d is representative of lectin pathway inhibition. With 2 mg/kg administered once weekly by intravenous infusion, C4d inhibition of approximately 90% was achieved after the first dose and later in the treatment (~Day 36); however, it was not maintained (&gt;80%) over each dosing period during the 6-week treatment period.
L1974: With 4 mg/kg administered once weekly by intravenous infusion, C4d inhibition of approximately 95% was achieved and a high level was maintained (&gt;80%) over each dosing period during the 6-week treatment period. Compared to 2 mg/kg IV, the higher 4 mg/kg once weekly IV dose exhibited greater C4d inhibition and was within the target to anticipate clinical benefit as per the Applicant.
L1975: Subcutaneous dosing regimens up to 8 mg/kg, 370 mg, 740 mg once weekly and 50 mg and 150 mg once daily were not able to maintain the same degree of persistent C4d inhibition as 4 mg/mL IV once weekly.
L1976: 14.3. Pharmacometrics Review
L1977: In general, the Applicant's population PK-PD analysis is considered acceptable in describing the observed narsoplimab-wuug concentrations in healthy subjects and patients. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1978: More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined below:
L1979: Table 101. Specific Comments on Applicant's Final Population PK and PD Model
L1980: Utility of the Final ModelReviewer's Comments




          Support Applicant's proposed labeling statements
          (b) (4)
        
          (b) (4)
        


          Derive exposure metrics for exposure-response analyses
        

• PK model was used to predict C_{min}, C_{max}, AUC.
• The PK-PD model was used to justify the selection of 4 mg/kg IV dosing in the Phase 2 studies as it was expected to maintain mean lectin pathway inhibition (using C4d as a marker) &gt;80% up to 168 hours (proposed dosing interval). Complement 4d inhibition (C4d) is a pharmacodynamic marker and not an efficacy endpoint in evaluating treatment response.
• The Applicant's final model is generally acceptable for generating exposure metrics. However, the PK modelling was not considered reliable for estimating the effect of plasma therapy on the drug's exposure.
• No association was observed between narsoplimab-wuug trough concentrations or the level of mean C4d inhibition and the response to narsoplimab-wuug treatment in patients with HSCT-TMA. Therefore, the mechanistic role of C4d inhibition to predict treatment response is not clear.
L1981: Utility of the Final ModelReviewer's Comments




          Predict exposures at alternative dosing regimen
          
No alternative dosing regimen were predicted via PK modeling.
The Applicant did not provide clinical justification or PK data (either through a dedicated study or PK modeling) to support the need for an additional narsoplimab-wuug dose of 2 mg/kg after each plasma therapy event.



The PK modelling was not considered reliable for predicting the effect of plasma therapy on the drug's exposure. Therefore, the need as well as the strength and frequency of supplemental dosing post-plasma exchange could not be reliably evaluated.
Although the reviewer's PK simulation did not show plasma exchange to affect narsoplimab-wuug exposure, the data collected in clinical studies to evaluate the impact of plasma exchange on narsoplimab-wuug clearance is not adequate. As plasma exchange, in general, is known to remove monoclonal antibodies from plasma, we agree with the recommendation of a supplemental dose of 2 mg/kg following each plasma exchange. The supplemental dose of 2 mg/kg was administered in clinical trials and the safety is established. Nevertheless, a dedicated PK study to evaluate the impact of plasma exchange on narsoplimab-wuug clearance should be considered as a postmarketing requirement to accurately assess the need and the amount of supplemental dose required post-plasma exchange.
L1982: Source: FDA Reviewer
L1983: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the plasma concentration-time curve; C_{max}, maximum plasma drug concentration; C_{min}, minimum plasma drug concentration; HSCT-TMA, hematopoietic stem cell transplant-associated thrombotic microangiopathy; IV, intravenous(i.v.); PD, pharmacodynamic; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1984: 14.3.1. Applicant's Population PK Analysis
L1985: The population PK (popPK) analysis was based on PK data from 4 studies (Table 102): Two Phase 1 studies in healthy subjects (OMS721-NHV-001 and OMS721-NHV-002) and two Phase 2 studies in different patients' populations (OMS721-GNP-001 and TMA-001) with thrombotic microangiopathies (TMAs) or glomerulopathies (GNPs). Narsoplimab-wuug was administered by either an SC injection or 30-minute IV infusion in healthy subjects. In Phase 2 studies, Narsoplimab-wuug was administered as 30-minute IV infusion. Only narsoplimab-wuug serum concentration data from the IV arms were used for the popPK analysis.
L1986: The analysis dataset comprised of 3330 PK observations from 110 subjects, including 159 below quantification limit concentrations. The planned timing of blood samples for narsoplimab-wuug, ADA, and inhibition of ex-vivo lectin-mediated complement pathway activation (inhibition of complement component 4d - C4d - formation) measurements are presented in Table 103.
L1987: The summary statistics for the continuous and categorical covariates are presented by study in Table 104. Twenty-eight adult patients with HSCT-TMA were enrolled in Study TMA-001 and during the whole clinical development program of narsoplimab-wuug.
L1988: Twenty-four patients with HSCT-TMA (median [range] weight =69 kg [51 – 134 kg]) received a narsoplimab-wuug dose of 4 mg/kg and 4 patients with HSCT-TMA (weight =71 kg [52 – 83 kg]) received a dose of 370 mg/kg (Figure 22).

L2005: Source: Reviewer's Summary from the Population PK datasets.
L2006: Base PK Model
L2007: The pharmacokinetics of narsoplimab-wuug following IV administration was described by a 2-compartment distribution model with parallel linear, and nonlinear Michaelis Menten elimination (Figure 23). The PK model was parameterized in terms of CL, central and peripheral volumes of distribution (V1 and V2 respectively), intercompartmental clearance (Q), maximum saturable elimination rate (V_{max}) and the Michaelis-Menten constant (K_m: Narsoplimab-wuug concentration that achieves half V_{max}). Between subject variability (BSV) was estimated on all parameters. The residual error model consisted of a combined additive and proportional error model.
L2008: Figure 23. Schematic of Structural PK Model for Narsoplimab-wuug
L2009: The diagram illustrates a two-compartment pharmacokinetic model. An 'infusion' input, characterized by 'AMT' (amount) and 'RATE', enters the 'central' compartment, which has a volume of distribution 'V1'. The 'central' compartment is connected to a 'periph.' (peripheral) compartment with volume 'V2' via an intercompartmental clearance 'Q'. Elimination from the 'central' compartment occurs through two pathways: a linear clearance 'CL' and a nonlinear Michaelis-Menten elimination characterized by 'Km' and 'Vmax'. The 'observation' is derived from the 'central' compartment and is influenced by 'add.err.' (additive error) and 'prop.err.' (proportional error).Schematic of Structural PK Model for Narsoplimab-wuug
L2010: Source: Applicant's Population PK Report, Figure 8, page 37.
L2011: Covariates Analysis
L2012: A stepwise covariate modeling procedure was used in order to build the covariate model, with a significance level of 0.05 for the forward inclusion and 0.001 for the backward deletion step.
L2013: The following covariates were included in the final model:
L2014: • Baseline Body Weight on CL using a power model, centered at 77.2 kg.• Albumin as a time varying covariate on CL using a proportional linear model, centered at 38 g/L.• Baseline albumin on V1 using a power model, centered at 36 g/L.• Dose on V1 using a power model, centered at 260 mg.• Antidrug antibody positive status as a time varying covariate on V_{max} (using linear proportional model).• Albumin as a time varying covariate on K_m using a power model, centered at 38 g/L.• Health status on K_m and V_{max} (using proportional linear model).
L2015: Body weight was not found to be a statistically significant covariate on the volumes of distribution. A total of 7 patients from Study TMA-001 (n=58) received plasma therapy (PLT) on the day of narsoplimab-wuug treatment or later during narsoplimab-wuug treatment, with 6 of 58 patients receiving plasma exchange and 1 patient receiving plasma infusion. The frequency of
L2016: plasma therapy varied between patients (Table 104). The effect of plasma therapy on narsoplimab-wuug CL was evaluated in the PK model and deemed not clinically significant.
L2017: Final Model
L2018: The parameter estimates from the final popPK model for narsoplimab-wuug are listed in Table 105. For an average 77.2 kg patient with a baseline albumin level of 36 g/L, an average albumin over time of 38 g/L, and no ADA, treated with 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug, the population estimates for narsoplimab-wuug CL, V1, V2, Q, Km and Vmax are 0.12 L/h (2.9 L/day); 10.9 L, 4.64 L, 0.021 L/h (0.5 L/day), 5705 ng/mL, and 0.346 mg/h (8.31 mg/day). The interindividual random effect (ETA) shrinkage from the final popPK model was 10.3%, 7.5%, 22.8%, 32.8%, 32.6%, and 40.9% for CL, V1, Q, V2, Km and Vmax.

L2021: a Estimates for between subject variability (BSV) parameters reported on SD scale.
L2022: Source: Applicant's Population PK Report, Table 14, page 43.
L2023: Albumin was identified as a major covariate explaining parts of the between subject variability on CL, V1, and Km. Further covariate effects were body weight on CL, dose on V1, ADA status on Vmax, and health status on Km and Vmax.
L2024: Most of patients who received plasma therapy on the day of treatment start or later (median plasma therapy duration of 1:75 h, ranging from 1 h to 2.87 h) received a supplemental narsoplimab-wuug dose of about 2 mg/kg after plasma therapy. The effect of plasma therapy on narsoplimab-wuug CL was never investigated in a dedicated study. Based on an information request, the Applicant assessed the effect of plasma therapy on CL using the developed final popPK model. The increase in narsoplimab-wuug clearance during plasma therapy was estimated to be 37.1% or 0.045 L/h for a typical patient. The modified popPK model could estimate the effect of plasma therapy on narsoplimab-wuug CL with reasonable precision (relative standard error of 19.9%), with a 95% confidence interval (CI) of the mean plasma effect ranging from 22.6% to 51.6%. However, the precision of the remaining PK parameters substantially decreased, indicating that the included outliers caused substantial numerical instabilities in the PK model. Based on a likelihood ratio test, the modified model was fitting the data significantly better (p-value=0.025) compared to the previously reported final popPK model. However, the addition of plasma therapy as covariate on CL did not reduce the unexplained BSV on CL. In addition, single 4 mg/kg dose PK simulations in a reference subject (70 kg, with median albumin concentrations and no ADA) without or with about 2 hours PLT after 24 hours of narsoplimab-wuug treatment initiation showed no noticeable difference in narsoplimab-wuug PK profile (Figure 24), with overlapping PK profiles. In view of these results the Applicant concluded that due to the modest effect size and short duration of PLT, no clinically meaningful effects of PLT on narsoplimab-wuug exposure could be identified that would support supplemental doses following plasma exchange.
L2025: The goodness-of-fit plots from the final popPK model (i.e., without PLT effect), are shown in Figure 25. The plots of the observed concentrations versus population predicted as well as the individual predicted concentrations show random normal scatter around the identity lines, indicating absence of systematic bias except for very few large concentrations (&gt;100 µg/mL) showing underprediction of these observations by the PK model. The population conditional and individual weighted residuals versus time after dose or versus population predicted concentrations also show random normal scatter around zero with no specific trend, indicating no apparent model misspecifications.
L2026: Figure 24. Simulation of Narsoplimab-wuug PK Profile After a Single 4 mg/kg Dose in a Typical 70 kg Patient With No ADA, Without and With Plasma Exchange
L2027: Figure 24: Simulation of Narsoplimab-wuug PK Profile. A semi-log plot showing Simulated Narsoplimab Conc. L2040: The nonparametric bootstrap (500 replicates), to assess the robustness of the final PK model, found that the deviation between the final model parameter estimates and the mean bootstrap estimates were small (-4.6% to 10.5%), and the bootstrap 95% CI did not include 0 and were close to the original CIs based on final PK model reported standard errors. Overall, the parameter estimates were considered robust (Table 106).

L2047: • The Applicant's PK model adequately describes the observed concentrations from narsoplimab-wuug clinical studies.
• The reviewer's evaluation of the model with the M3 method to handle values below quantification limit did not improve the model or affected the PK parameter estimates.
• The reviewer's prediction-corrected visual predictive check (pcVPC) shows that the model reasonably captured and predicted the observed mean PK profiles and the between-subject variability of narsoplimab-wuug concentrations, with the 5th, 50th and 95th percentiles of the prediction-corrected observed concentrations (red solid lines, Figure 26) are either overlaying with the 5th, 50th and 95th percentiles of the prediction-corrected simulated concentrations (black dashed lines) or encompassed within the confidence
L2048: intervals of the 5th, 50th and 95th percentiles of the prediction-corrected simulated concentrations (shaded area, Figure 26).

L2055: Adequacy of the Proposed Dosing to Standardize Exposure Between Patients Over the Body Weight Range
L2056: The proposed dosing of 4 mg/kg of narsoplimab-wuug capped to 370 mg is considered appropriate, as it provides comparable steady-state exposure (area under the concentration-time curve over the dosing interval: AUC_{ss}, maximum concentration: C_{max}, and trough concentration: C_{min}) across the body weight range. Figure 27 shows the predicted steady-state exposure metrics from PK simulations using the clinical studies population and the estimated individual PK parameters from the PK model. The red boxplots represent the exposure metrics if all subjects received a 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug dose (i.e., without dose capping) and the blue boxplots represent the exposure metrics if all subjects received a fixed narsoplimab-wuug dose of 370 mg.
L2057: The PK simulations demonstrate that capping narsoplimab-wuug dosing to 370 mg, particularly in heavier subjects (weight &gt;100 kg), provides consistent and comparable narsoplimab-wuug exposure (mainly C_{max,ss} and AUC_{ss}) to subjects with body weight below 92.5 kg and receiving 4 mg/kg of narsoplimab-wuug (red boxplots).
L2058: The administration of a 4 mg/kg narsoplimab-wuug dose in subjects with body weight above 100 kg (red boxplot) or the administration of a fixed dose of 370 mg in subjects with body weight below 70 kg (blue boxplot) is expected to result in relatively higher exposure compared to the studied and current proposed dosing.
L2059: Figure 27. Predicted Narsoplimab-wuug AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss}, and C_{min,ss} Under 4 mg/kg and 370 mg in the Studies Population
L2060: Figure 27: Two boxplots comparing predicted steady-state exposure metrics (AUC_ss and C_max,ss) for narsoplimab-wuug across a body weight range (45-135 kg). The left plot shows AUC_ss (ug*h/mL) and the right plot shows C_max,ss (ug/mL). Both plots compare two dosing regimens: 4 mg/kg (red boxplots) and 370 mg (blue boxplots). A vertical dashed line at 92.5 kg separates the data. In the AUC_ss plot, the 4 mg/kg dose generally results in higher exposure than the 370 mg dose for weights below 92.5 kg, while the 370 mg dose results in higher exposure for weights above 92.5 kg. The C_max,ss plot shows a similar pattern, with the 4 mg/kg dose resulting in higher exposure for weights below 92.5 kg and the 370 mg dose resulting in higher exposure for weights above 92.5 kg.
