L2448: 18. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance: Additional Information and Assessment
L2449: Narsoplimab-wuug is a monoclonal antibody that binds to and blocks MASP-2, the effector enzyme of the lectin pathway of complement. Further review of the mechanism of action of narsoplimab-wuug was undertaken by the clinical team.
L2450: An information request was sent to the Applicant to further evaluate the mechanism of action in terms of the HSCT-TMA. The Applicant provided an additional document pertaining to the mechanism of action (MOA) of narsoplimab-wuug for the treatment of HSCT-TMA on March 19, 2021. The Applicant also submitted responses to additional information requests (IRs) on August 4, 2021, August 6, 2021 and August 12, 2021 which were also reviewed.
L2451: The Applicant states that there are several different lines of evidence that provide confirmatory support of the mechanism of action of the drug to include in vitro models of HSCT-TMA that demonstrate that narsoplimab-wuug inhibits complement deposition, endothelial cell injury, and apoptosis. In addition, the Applicant provided data that the lectin pathway involvement in HSCT-TMA is demonstrated by immunohistochemistry findings of deposition of lectin pathway components in affected tissue of patients with HSCT-TMA.
L2452: The Applicant cites that separate studies of ongoing immunohistochemical analyses from skin biopsies of patients with persistent HSCT-TMA show positive staining for MASP-2, the effector enzyme of the lectin pathway of complement in 6 of 7 patients (Gayriilaki et al. 2017). In patients who did not develop TMA, there was no MASP-2 staining in the biopsies suggesting that TMA-related complement activation involves the lectin pathway. The Applicant states that by blocking MASP-2, narsoplimab-wuug blocks activation and complement activation.
L2453: In plasma samples from patients with acute TMA arising from a variety of clinical settings to include aHUS, HSCT-TMA, narsoplimab-wuug showed evidence of blocking microvascular endothelial cell apoptosis. In this model of microvascular injury, primary human microvascular endothelial cells grown in tissue culture media were exposed to control plasma or plasma from individual patients with active HSCT-TMA, in the presence of narsoplimab-wuug or isotype-matched control. Endothelial injury was evaluated by quantification of caspase 8 activity.
L2454: Caspase 8 activation was observed in 14 out of the 22 TMA patient's plasma. Blockade of MASP-2 by narsoplimab-wuug led to a decrease in caspase 8 activity for all 14 patient plasma suggesting that narsoplimab-wuug suppressed TMA plasma-mediated caspase 8 activation in microvascular endothelial cells. This suggests that there is a role in for narsoplimab-wuug to block MASP-2 however it is not known how much of this blockade impacts the downstream signaling of the complement cascade.
L2455: The Applicant also provided data from an ex-vivo assay using HSCT-TMA patient serum and the effect of narsoplimab-wuug. Serum from patients with acute HSCT-TMA had higher complement (C5b-9) deposition on human microvascular endothelial cells and this response was suppressed by addition of narsoplimab-wuug (Galbusera M et al. 2021). However, this is ex-vivo data and it is unclear how much of suppressive response narsoplimab-wuug induces.
L2456: The Applicant provided data indicating that host responses to COVID-19 share aspects of HSCT-TMA pathophysiology which can be improved upon with the use of narsoplimab-wuug. According to the Applicant, COVID-19 and HSCT-TMA share a similar pathophysiology and are endothelial injury syndromes. MASP-2 has been described in lung injury in patients with coronavirus infection with MASP-2 and mannan-binding lectin staining in immunohistochemical analysis of lung tissues from patients who died of COVID-19 (Magro et al. 2020). In addition, microvascular deposits of MASP-2, C4d, C3d, and C5b-9 in some organ systems have been described (Magro et al. 2020). The Applicant provided data from an initial cohort of 9 patients with COVID-19 treated with narsoplimab-wuug under a compassionate use program. According to the Applicant, patients had elevated circulating endothelial cells counts at baseline, which decreased after the first week of narsoplimab-wuug. An additional cohort of 10 patients was treated with narsoplimab-wuug under compassionate use. Eight of 10 patients recovered and survived and were discharged following 5-8 doses of narsoplimab-wuug. The D-dimer levels rapidly normalized following administration of narsoplimab-wuug and correspond with clinical status/recovery. The Applicant states that severe COVID-19 is pathogenetically similar to HSCT-TMA. Narsoplimab-wuug shows effects on TMA-associated pathophysiological processes and clinical outcomes in critically ill patients with COVID-19.
L2457: Brief Summary of Nonclinical Data (Taken From Summary by Pharmacology-Toxicology Review Team)
L2458: TMA Study in Mice
L2459: In vivo efficacy of OMS721 was assessed in a mouse model of TMA in which localized thrombosis was induced by photo-activation of fluorescein isothiocyanate-labeled dextran in the microvasculature of the cremaster muscle of C57Bl/6 mice.
L2460: Administering OMS721 one hour before induction of thrombosis delayed the initiation of thrombus formation and the time to complete vessel occlusion, demonstrating a contribution of the lectin complement pathway to the thrombotic response to injury. However, over time, thrombus formation still occurred in a similar percentage of mice regardless of treatment, suggesting that pathways in addition to lectin complement activation contribute to and eventually result in microvascular thrombosis.
L2461: Human aHUS Study
L2462: Serum was taken from healthy people or from aHUS patients and incubated with endothelial cells in culture. MAC (C5b-9) deposition and platelet adherence to the EC were subsequently measured.
L2463: • Serum from aHUS patients robustly caused MAC deposition and platelet adherence to ECs.• Narsoplimab partially prevented MAC deposition and platelet adhesion, indicating some contribution of lectin, but also showing contribution by other C-activation pathways.
L2464: Narsoplimab is specific for MASP-2 and selectively blocks lectin-initiated complement activation. The lectin pathway contributes to C-activation and thrombus formation in defined models of endothelial injury, which presumably reflect a key element of TMA. Narsoplimab inhibited this contribution. However, other C-activation pathways are also contributory; thus, vascular occlusion appeared delayed, but occlusion still occurred.
L2465: There is insufficient mechanistic evidence to provide confirmatory evidence for this application. There does appear to be sufficient literature to document the presence of MASP-2 and lectin components in TMA and HSCT-TMA. However, the Applicant has not provided mechanistic data to demonstrate a beneficial clinical outcome in TMA by blocking MASP-2. Although there appears to be partial mitigation of C-activation and a delay in thrombotic onset by narsoplimab-wuug in animal and in vitro models, the Applicant has not shown sufficient evidence that this translates into a measurable clinical benefit, either directly or indirectly. It is possible that narsoplimab-wuug's inhibition of the lectin pathway is not sufficient to alter the outcome in HSCT-TMA in a sufficient magnitude to demonstrate clinical benefit.
L2466: 19. Other Drug Development Considerations:Additional Information and Assessment
L2467: Not applicable.
L2468: 20. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office ofScientific Investigations, OtherInspections)
L2469: The Division requested that Office of Scientific Investigations inspect a clinical study clinical investigator site, and the Applicant were inspected in support of BLA 761152. Based on these inspections the conduct of the Study TMA-001 appears to be adequate. The study data derived from the clinical investigator's site and the Applicant are considered reliable.
L2470: For the clinical investigator site, a FDA Form 483, Inspectional Observations, was issued at the end of the inspections for failure to prepare or maintain adequate case histories with respect to observations and data pertinent to the investigation. Specifically, a Subject (b) (6) had a serious
L2471: adverse event (worsening hospitalization for acute Graft Versus Host Disease) that was not reported immediately.
L2472: Subject (b) (6) had three adverse events that were not recorded to an electronic data capture system in a timely manner. The adverse events were coagulase negative staphylococcus bacteremia due to a central line infection, metabolic acidosis secondary to renal dysfunction and elevated alkaline phosphatase levels.
L2473: The response from the clinical site to FDA Form 483, dated January 12, 2021, appeared adequate. Although the above findings are regulatory violations, these adverse events were included in the submission and these findings do not appear to have significant impact on the safety assessment of the study.
L2474: In terms of the Applicant site audit, no regulatory deficiencies were observed during the inspection and no Form FDA-483, Inspectional Observations were issued.

L2485: 23. Financial Disclosure
L2486: The Applicant provided certification on FDA Form 3454 to indicate that there were no financial arrangements between the Applicant and Investigators that could be affected by the outcome of Study TMA-001. The Applicant did not provide the number of investigators and an IR was sent to the Applicant in which the Applicant stated that the total number of investigators identified was 163. They also stated that two of the 163 investigators received payments that reached a cumulative monetary value that exceeded $25,000 and that this occurred in 2021. The value did not exceed $35,000. Per 21 CFR 54.2 significant payments of other sorts includes cumulative monetary value that exceeds $25,000. The Applicant provided Form FDA 3455 for each investigator that included the disclosable financial interest/arrangements and a description of the steps taken to minimize potential bias.
